# FPAI Web App — Design Discussions & User Stories

This document tracks the third FPAI component: a bettor-facing web app (`app/`) built on top of the ML forecasting engine (`FPAI_PRD.md`) and the LangGraph betting agent (`agent_prd.md`). Stories are prefixed `W##` to distinguish them from the ML forecasting engine (`US#`) and agent (`A##`) stories.

**Status:** functionality is still being worked out. Unlike the other two story documents, this one leads with an open discussion log. Nothing in the Open Design Discussions section is a decision — items only graduate into the Stories table once we've actually agreed on them. `documents/app_prd.md` and `documents/app_techspec.md` are drafts and will be filled in as these discussions resolve.

---

## Confirmed So Far

These came out of initial brainstorming and are treated as settled unless something below reopens them:

- **Primary user:** an individual bettor (not an internal analyst dashboard) — same user described in `agent_prd.md` Section 2.1.
- **Tech stack:** Next.js/React + TypeScript frontend, FastAPI backend wrapping the existing Python agent/forecast code.
- **Auth:** single-user, no accounts, for the MVP.
- **Bet tracker:** logs bets the user actually placed (not automatic hypothetical tracking).
- **"Why" explanation:** expandable static detail from the already-generated payload — no new on-demand LLM call.
- **Directory:** new top-level `app/` (vs. `frontend/`, `webapp/`, `dashboard/`).
- **Fixture discovery (D1):** fixtures are fetched from an external fixtures/results API (which provider is still open — see D1a below), and each fetched match is sent to the agent to generate a recommendation. No manual home/away/date entry as the primary flow.
- **Recommendation generation (D2):** the agent generates a recommendation (including its existing natural-language explanation) for every match displayed in the app. No follow-up/interactive Q&A from the user — one generation per match, that's it.
- **"Why" panel scope (D4):** pure text. No charts, no structured widgets, no interactivity — just the explanation (and other text fields like confidence/limitations) rendered as text.
- **Bet tracker lifecycle (D3, partial):** user selects a match in the app and records the bet they placed against it (market/selection/stake/odds); the app later fetches the actual match result and auto-records the outcome (via the same fixtures/results API as D1). Exact field list and status values still to be nailed down — see D3a.
- **Backend/engine boundary (D5):** fixture discovery, recommendation orchestration/caching, and bet tracking logic all live in `app/backend` as app-specific glue. `src/agent` and `src/forecast` are unchanged, called as libraries.
- **Local dev practice (D7):** standard two-process local dev — Next.js dev server (`npm run dev`) and FastAPI via `uvicorn --reload`, talking over HTTP with CORS (or a Next.js rewrite proxy) rather than a shared process. No Docker for the prototype phase; revisit containerization only if this moves beyond local dev.
- **Fixtures/results provider (D1a):** `football-data.org`. Used both to discover upcoming fixtures (D1) and to fetch actual results for bet auto-settlement (D3). Free-tier rate limits will need to be respected by the caching/orchestration layer (see D2a).
- **Visual design (D6):** dark mode only (locked, not auto/light), Tailwind CSS with hand-rolled components (no design-system library like shadcn/Radix), high visual density with card-based match displays, real club-color identity badges (config-driven — see `TEAM_COLORS` in the sandbox), system-ui sans, Phosphor icons, plain CSS transitions (150ms) for hover/expand — no animation library. Validated in the `sandbox/` prototype across all three pages (Dashboard, Match Explorer, Match Analysis) before being ported into the real `app/frontend`.
- **Recommendation generation timing (D2a):** not on-demand/on-page-load. Two scheduled triggers instead: (1) an end-of-day batch, once daily at end-of-day America/New_York time (handles EST/EDT correctly via `zoneinfo`, not a fixed UTC offset — exact trigger time TBD, proposed 23:00), which discovers the next day's fixtures and generates+caches an initial recommendation for each; (2) a per-match refresh 30 minutes before kickoff, re-running the agent with freshly-fetched odds since odds tend to settle close to kickoff. The dashboard/API always reads from the cache in the normal path — it does not call `run_agent()` synchronously on a user request. A manual "regenerate now" escape hatch remains for dev/testing and late-added fixtures.
- **Odds data source (D2b):** The Odds API, free tier (~500 credits/month), scoped tightly — 1X2 market only, single region — for E0 fixtures only (our only real-data-coverage league today anyway). football-data.org (D1a) turned out to have **no odds on its free tier at all** (verified: odds are a paid add-on, €15/month, requiring a paid base plan too — €27+/month minimum) — it remains the fixtures/results provider, but odds now come from a second, separate provider. The scheduled odds refresh (D2a) must degrade gracefully (skip, keep last-known odds) if the monthly credit budget is exhausted rather than erroring.
- **Bet tracker field list & logging paths (D3a):** every logged bet records date, home/away team, the bet (market + selection), odds, outcome (`open` → `won`/`lost`; void/push deferred), stake, and profit/loss (`stake × (odds − 1)` if won, `−stake` if lost, `null` while open). Two ways to log a bet: (1) **from a recommendation** — user picks a recommendation card and every field is auto-filled and locked except stake, which is the only input; the full recommendation JSON is snapshotted at log time (recommendations aren't reproducible run-to-run — agent_techspec.md §18.6); (2) **manual entry** — user selects a real fixture via the Match Explorer's existing search (not freeform team-name typing, so auto-settlement still works) and fills in market/selection/odds/stake themselves; no recommendation snapshot for this path.
- **Agent invocation ownership (2026-07-11):** the app is always the party that initiates a call into the agent — via `W02`'s `run_agent()` wrapper, on the app's own schedule (`W09`/`W10`) or through its manual "regenerate now" path. The agent side never independently drives production batch orchestration for the app. Concretely: `A18` (`agent-batch` CLI, `agent_user_stories.md` Phase 6), if it's ever built, is a standalone tool for someone using the agent outside the app — the app does not call it, wait for it, or treat it as a prerequisite. `W19`'s batch/weekend view, if built, is a UI layer over recommendations `W09`/`W11` already generated and cached — not a call-out to `A18`. This resolves the "which side owns batch recommendations" question raised in Phase 5/6 of both story docs.
- **Frontend canonical timezone (2026-07-18):** production's frontend "now" (used for date-window queries like Match Explorer's fixture search and `formatDay`'s real-clock fallback) is standardized to America/New_York (Eastern Time) rather than the browser's ambient local timezone, matching the backend's existing `NY_TZ` convention throughout `app/backend/scheduler.py` — tracked as **W40**. Testing overrides continue to use the existing sandbox clock mechanism (W27) unchanged; this decision only changes what the *non-sandboxed* default resolves to.

## Open Design Discussions

D1, D1a, D2, D2a, D2b, D3, D3a, D4, D5, D6, D7 are all resolved (see Confirmed So Far above). Items below came out of a three-persona UI critique (2026-08-13 — traditional sports-app users, sports-betting-app users, social-media-only users; see PHASE 25 below for the stories that came out of the same critique but didn't need a scope decision first). These need a product decision before they can become real stories — sized/scoped speculative work isn't worth committing to code yet:

- **D8 — Real team crest imagery.** Persona 1 (sports-app) and persona 3 (social-media) both read the current initials-badge (`TeamBadge`) as a placeholder rather than a design choice. D6 deliberately locked "real club-color identity badges" as the chosen visual language — swapping to real crests would reopen that decision, and needs a licensing/sourcing answer (which crest image source, at what cost/rate-limit) before it's a scoped story.
- **D9 — Live scores / standings / head-to-head context.** Persona 1 expects this by default from an ESPN/Sofascore mental model; today FPAI shows neither. This reopens `app_prd.md` §5's non-goals rather than being a quick add — is FPAI staying a pre-match analysis tool, or growing a live-scores surface? Needs a product-scope answer, not a UI story.
- **D10 — Multi-book odds comparison / line-movement history.** Persona 2 (betting-app) expects both; D2b's Odds API integration is single-provider, single-region, 1X2-market-only on a ~500-credit/month free tier. Same class of question as W24 (already-open exploration of odds providers) — comparison/history needs a materially bigger data budget or a different provider, not a frontend change.
- **D11 — First-run onboarding walkthrough.** Considered and deferred (YAGNI) for now — W107/W111 below (inline tooltips + a plain-language summary line) are the cheap version of the same problem for persona 3. Only worth a real walkthrough if those measurably don't close the gap.

---

## Integration Gaps & Concerns (Agent + ML Engine)

Found while planning the real-backend integration (2026-07-10), by reading `agent_techspec.md`/`FRAI_TECHSPEC.md` and verifying the claims directly against the running code and the live DuckDB store — several things below are code-verified, not just doc-derived, and the docs occasionally disagree with what the code actually does. Grouped by who owns the fix.

**At-a-glance status** (detail for each is in the prose below — "Planned" means a story now exists, not that it's built yet):

| # | Concern | Category | Status | Tracked In |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Non-EPL fixtures silently degrade to a mislabeled cold-start forecast (league-routing gap) | App-side | 🟡 App-side mitigated; root cause tracked cross-team | W03 · US#107 · A27 |
| 15 | Swedish Allsvenskan (`SWE`) is fully built engine-side (data, models, registry) but the app never requests it — hardcoded allowlist + odds/fixture defaults | App-side | 🔧 Planned, scoped | W55–W63 (Phase 12) |
| 2 | football-data.org team names vs. the engine's canonical names | App-side | 🟡 Draft mapping done; 6 of 34 unresolved | W06 · `football_data_org_team_mapping_draft.json` |
| 3 | Recommendation generation is slow (10-30s) and costly per match | App-side | ✅ Resolved by design | D2a · W08–W11 |
| 4 | football-data.org has no odds on its free tier | App-side | ✅ Resolved — second provider added | D2b · W07 |
| 5 | `direct_bet` with `current_odds: null` | App-side | 🔧 Planned fix | BUG-013 · A28 |
| 6 | `extract_recommendation` doesn't validate value types, only key presence | App-side | 🔧 Planned fix | A28 |
| 7 | `min_odds_threshold`/`max` not code-enforced | Cross-team | 🔧 Planned fix, bounds also widened | A29 |
| 8 | Corners markets can't be auto-scored | Cross-team | ⏸️ Accepted, deferred indefinitely | excluded in W13 |
| 9 | LLM output isn't reproducible run-to-run | Cross-team | 🟡 Partially mitigated | W10 (skip-if-unchanged) · W12 (snapshot at log time) |
| 10 | Result-leakage defenses are a mitigation, not a guarantee | Cross-team | ⏸️ Accepted, deferred | — |
| 11 | Needs a local Ollama daemon (or Anthropic key) to run at all | Operational | ℹ️ Confirmed as accepted, not a gap | — |
| 12 | `raw_matches` staleness | Operational | ✅ Confirmed non-issue right now (off-season) + future-proofed | US#109 |
| 13 | Backend must run continuously for the scheduler to fire | Operational | ✅ Addressed via build sequencing | W08–W10 moved to build-last milestone |
| 14 | Odds API free-tier budget is tight | Operational | ✅ Resolved (counter) + exploring alternatives | W07 (counter) · W24 (explore) |

### App-side (we mitigate these ourselves — see stories below)

- **Only Premier League has real team-history data — update (2026-07-23): Sweden's Allsvenskan is now a second exception.** Original finding (still true for every other league): `SELECT league, COUNT(*) FROM raw_matches` returned exactly one row: `('E0', 3800, max_date=2026-05-24)`; `config/competitions.yaml` registered exactly two competitions, `E0` (`competition_specific`) and a generic `international` (`general_purpose`) — no La Liga, Serie A, Bundesliga, or Champions League. Since then, a third `competition_specific` registration has been added and fully built out: `SWE` (Allsvenskan) — `raw_matches` now has 3,489 SWE rows through 2026-07-20, and `config/model_selection.yaml` has a trained/selected `SWE` model context. This was built on the ML-engine side, tracked outside this doc (`documents/user_stories.md`), not as an app-side (`W##`) story. The app-side boundary is still narrow, just one league wider than before: it's "Premier League and Allsvenskan vs literally everything else," and per Phase 12 (W55–W63) the app doesn't yet route to `SWE` even though the engine now supports it.
- **Calling the league-history path for a non-EPL match doesn't fail cleanly — it silently degrades.** Verified by reading `ForecastService.forecast_upcoming()` (`src/forecast/forecast_service.py:314-429`) directly: the `match_type != "international"` branch **never consults `config/competitions.yaml`** — it unconditionally calls `FeatureFactory.build_for_match(league=league, ...)` and unconditionally loads the flat `"league"` model context (trained solely on E0 data), regardless of what `league` string is passed. A Real Madrid fixture routed this way gets `prediction_basis: "team_history_and_market"` and `caveat: "Full team history and market features used."` even though every rolling/team feature is cold-start-zero-filled — the only honest signal left is `cold_start_risk: true` (completeness < 0.85), buried in diagnostics, not in the headline label. `FRAI_TECHSPEC.md` §27.2 describes the competitions registry as resolving tier for "named competitions" — that wiring exists for training-time tier resolution (`model_manager.py`, `main.py`) but **not** for this live spot-inference path. Doc/code mismatch, not a misunderstanding on our part.
- **The agent's own tool selection can't be trusted to catch this either.** `config/prompts/agent_v1.txt` line 17: *"CALL forecast_league (domestic) or forecast_international (international/unknown)"* — a coarse LLM judgment call, not a registry lookup. La Liga is domestic, so the LLM has every reason to call `forecast_league` for a Real Madrid match, hitting the exact silent-degradation path above.
- **Mitigation (W03 below):** the app must decide `match_info["league"]` itself before calling `run_agent()` — pass `"E0"` only for actual Premier League fixtures (confirmed via a hardcoded allowlist for now, since the registry isn't consulted at the layer that would need it), and omit `league` entirely for everything else. `agent_prd.md`'s own CLI docs already establish this convention ("Omit `--league` for international fixtures — agent uses `forecast_international`"), so this isn't a new pattern, just enforcing an existing one at the app boundary since the engine won't enforce it for us.
- **Team-name mismatches between football-data.org and the ML engine.** `config/team_mapping.json` + `TeamNameMapper` (now source-agnostic per `FRAI_TECHSPEC.md` §27.3's ingestion restructuring) already handle Understat/FotMob naming variants — reusable, not a rebuild — but football-data.org's own naming conventions haven't been added to it. A mismatch here means `FeatureFactory.build_for_match` silently returns a cold-start row for a team that *does* have real history, just under a name the mapper doesn't recognize yet.
- **Recommendation generation is slow and not free — resolved via scheduling (D2a), not caching-on-first-request.** `run_agent()` is a real LLM (Ollama or Anthropic) + one Tavily search call per match, 10-30s per agent_techspec.md §7. Rendering "today's edges" for N fixtures live would mean N of these on a page load; instead, generation happens on two schedules (end-of-day batch, per-match T-30 refresh) using the concurrent, fault-tolerant pattern already proven in `_run_backtest_concurrent()` (§13), and the dashboard only ever reads from cache. This trades the "slow on page load" problem for a new one: **the backend process must stay running continuously** for the schedule to actually fire — see Operational below.
- **football-data.org has no odds on its free tier (D1a/D2b).** Verified against the pricing page directly: the Free tier is scores/fixtures/schedules/tables only; "Pre-Match Home/Draw/Away odds" is a separate €15/month add-on that itself requires a paid base plan. This is more than an odds-freshness nuance — without real odds, `run_agent()`'s workflow (agent_techspec.md §5: forecast tool called *before* web_search, using "explicit fallback odds defaults if none provided") means `value_edge`/`implied_probability` would be computed against placeholder numbers, not the LLM reliably searching out real current odds first. A second provider (The Odds API, D2b) is now required, not optional.
- **`direct_bet` with `current_odds: null` is a real, observed agent output** (agent_techspec.md §18.3 — this crashed the staking simulator before it was patched there). Our UI/API layer needs its own defensive handling, independent of that fix, since we're a new consumer of the same loosely-typed output.
- **`extract_recommendation` only validates key presence, not value types** (agent_techspec.md §8, §17) — a market could come back with `value_edge: "high"` (a string) and pass the agent's own validation. Our FastAPI layer needs its own Pydantic validation with graceful degradation (flag/hide the malformed market), not blind trust that the agent's schema guarantees hold.

### Cross-team (agent/ML-engine-side; app can work around but not fix)

- **`min_odds_threshold`/`min_value_edge` are prompt-level instructions, not code-enforced** (agent_techspec.md §17, confirmed still true) — nothing stops the LLM from emitting `direct_bet` below the 2.0 floor. **Resolved (2026-07-11):** bounds widened and made code-enforced — decimal odds must fall in `[1.2, 11.0]` (American −500 to +1000) for a `direct_bet`, not just `>= 2.0`. Tracked as `agent_user_stories.md` A29 (Phase 10), which adds a `max_odds_threshold` config field (didn't exist before — only a floor did) and enforces both bounds in code rather than prompt-only. The app should still not blindly trust the bound holds for every historical/cached recommendation generated before A29 ships.
- **Corners markets (`home_corners`/`away_corners`) cannot be auto-scored.** `_market_correct()` (agent_techspec.md §11.1) always returns `None` for them — there's no numeric line field on `MarketRecommendation`, only the free-text `selection` string (e.g. `"over_4.5"`), which isn't reliably parseable across runs. **Status: accepted for now, revisit later (2026-07-11)** — closing this properly requires a schema change to `MarketRecommendation` (a numeric line field), which isn't worth the engineering investment yet. Corners stay excluded from auto-settlement (W13) indefinitely until this is revisited, not just "for v1."
- **LLM output isn't reproducible run-to-run** (agent_techspec.md §18.6 — snapshot replay key misses from LLM-regenerated tool arguments; separately, final recommendations vary run-to-run even in live mode). This matters for us specifically because a user's logged bet should be tied to *the recommendation they actually saw*, not a fresh regeneration — confirms the bet tracker must snapshot the full `MatchRecommendation` at log time. **Partially mitigated by design (2026-07-11):** W10's T-30 refresh now only re-runs `run_agent()` when the odds have actually changed since the last generation (see W10/W11 below) — minimizing unnecessary re-invocations reduces how often this non-reproducibility is even exercised, though it doesn't make a single re-run deterministic.
- **Result-leakage defenses are a mitigation, not a guarantee** (agent_techspec.md §18.7 — one pilot match leaked a real result into a "historical" agent run despite two layers of defense). **Status: accepted for now, revisit later (2026-07-11)** — low relevance for live recommendations on genuinely upcoming fixtures today, but worth a closer look before this app grows a "review past agent calls" feature using `SnapshotStore`, or before the agent-side snapshot corpus scales up for other reasons.

### Operational

- **The forecast engine needs a local Ollama daemon (or an Anthropic API key) to answer anything.** **Confirmed decision (2026-07-11): local LLM (Ollama) for now**, revisit only if that changes later. Default `config/agent_config.yaml` is `provider: ollama`, `model: llama3.1:8b` — running the FastAPI backend requires Ollama installed and running on the same machine. Not currently called out anywhere as an app setup prerequisite — worth adding to a setup doc when one exists.
- **`raw_matches` was last refreshed 2026-05-24.** **Re-checked (2026-07-11) — this is not neglect, it's the football calendar.** `data/raw/football_data/E0_2526.csv` was actually re-scraped as recently as 2026-07-05 (file mtime), but the max match date in `raw_matches` is still 2026-05-24 because that's genuinely the end of the 2025/26 Premier League season — there's nothing newer to fetch until pre-season/2026/27 fixtures start being played (~August). The underlying concern still stands once the new season starts: nothing runs `python main.py refresh-data` on a schedule today. **Resolved as a story:** `documents/user_stories.md` Phase 17, US#109 — weekly scheduled refresh of all scraped data.
- **The backend must run continuously for D2a's scheduled jobs to fire at all.** Closing the laptop or stopping `uvicorn` means the end-of-day batch and any pending T-30 refreshes silently don't happen. A single-user local prototype doesn't need 24/7 uptime guarantees, but the scheduler should detect missed windows on startup (e.g. no batch ran for today) and catch up rather than silently staying empty until the next scheduled fire. **Sequencing decision (2026-07-11):** the scheduler (W08/W09/W10) is deliberately built *last*, right before going live — see the note at the top of Story Dependencies below.
- **The Odds API's free tier is genuinely tight.** ~500 credits/month, and a live-odds call costs `markets × regions` credits. Scoped to 1 market × 1 region for E0-only fixtures (D2b), this comfortably covers a season's worth of EOD + T-30 calls today, but there's no slack for adding markets, regions, or leagues later without hitting a paid tier. **Resolved (2026-07-11):** a usage counter is now explicit in W07's acceptance criteria (not just "tracks usage" vaguely). Separately, **exploring a better-fitting odds API is now tracked as a future story (W24)** rather than assumed settled — The Odds API was chosen as the free, zero-friction starting point, not necessarily the long-term answer.

---

## Story Dependencies & Execution Order

> **Sequencing decision (2026-07-11): the scheduler is the last feature built before going live.** W08/W09/W10 are listed in Phase 2 below because that's where they functionally belong (fixture/odds discovery), but their *build order* is deliberately last, after Phase 6 (Testing). Every other story — recommendation generation, bet tracking, UI — is developed and tested against W11's manual "regenerate now" path; the actual automated EOD/T-30 scheduling only gets wired up and left running right before launch. Their Milestone labels reflect this (M7, not M2), even though their dependency edges below are unchanged.

```
PHASE 1 (Backend Foundation & Single-Match Wiring)
  W01 → W02 → W03 → W04

PHASE 2 (Fixture, Odds Discovery & Scheduled Batch Generation)
  W05 → W06
  W07 (independent)
  W07 → W25 (independent, blocked on an Odds API key -- W09's live smoke test still benefits from this before going live, but W09 itself doesn't hard-depend on it)
  W08 (independent — built last, see note above)
  W02 → W11
  W02 + W05 + W06 + W07 + W08 + W11 → W09 (built last, see note above)
  W07 + W09 + W11 → W10 (built last, see note above)

PHASE 3 (Bet Tracker & Settlement)
  W04 + W11 → W12
  W05 + W12 → W13
  W13 → W14

PHASE 4 (Trust & Data-Quality Surfacing)
  W04 + W03 → W15
  W02 + W15 → W16

PHASE 5 (Future Extensions)
  W11 → W17 (independent, low priority)
  — → W18 (blocked on ML-engine-side work, not app work)
  W09 → W19 (UI view only over W09/W11's own cache — never delegates to A18, see "Agent invocation ownership" above)
  W07 → W24 (independent, exploratory — no rush while free tier suffices)

PHASE 6 (Testing & Release Readiness)
  W02 + W12 + W13 → W20
  W08 + W09 + W10 → W21
  W04 → W22
  W20 + W21 + W22 → W23
  W23 + W25 → W26 (blocked on timing -- a real match completing live -- and W25's real Odds API key)

PHASE 7 (Sandbox / Point-in-Time Testing Environment -- 2026-07-16 addition)
  W27 (independent, foundational -- an app-wide overridable clock)
  W07 (completed) + BUG-015 (fixed) → W28 (independent of W27 -- a real historical odds source)
  W27 + W28 → W29 (wires both into the backend + isolates sandbox data from real dev data)
  W27 → W30 (frontend clock awareness -- independently buildable, needs W29 to verify live end-to-end)
  W27 + W28 + W29 + W30 → W31 (the repeatable runbook that exercises all of the above together)
  W28 → W32 (future, deferred -- odds-movement is a separate, dynamic scenario, not part of this batch)

PHASE 8 (Time-Related Correctness Testing -- 2026-07-16 addition)
  -- No dependency on Phase 7's sandbox stories; independently buildable now: --
  W08 + W09 + W10 + W21 (all completed) → W33 (multi-day scheduler soak test + DST correctness)
  W17 (completed) → W34 (inject a clock into raw_matches staleness -- has no seam today, blocking)
  W07 (completed) → W35 (credit-counter persistence across a real month-boundary restart)
  W05 (completed) → W36 (rate-limiter multi-step sequence test)
  W08 (completed) → W39 (does the still-disconnected weekly data-refresh scheduler, US#109, coexist safely with W08's if ever run in the same process)
  -- Gated on Phase 7 landing first -- these test sandbox-only code paths: --
  W27 + W29 → W37 (wires SnapshotStore record/replay into the sandbox agent path -- the C fix; cannot start before W27/W29 exist)
  W30 → W38 (frontend date-boundary correctness, using the sandbox clock as the vehicle -- cannot start before W30 exists)

PHASE 9 (Frontend Timezone Standardization & Scheduler Live-Fire Coverage -- 2026-07-18 addition)
  W40 (independent -- standardizes frontend "now" to America/New_York, matching the backend's existing NY_TZ convention)
  W08 (completed) → W41 (scheduler live-fire smoke test for CronTrigger, distinct from the catch-up logic already covered by W21/W33)

PHASE 10 (Bugs Found By Actually Running The App -- 2026-07-18 addition)
  W30 + W38 (completed) → W42 (fixture-fetch race condition -- stale response can overwrite fresh sandbox data)
  W37 (completed) → W43 (snapshot replay key-miss should degrade gracefully, not 500)
  W31 (completed) → W44 (future -- automate the manual preflight/drive/verify workflow that found W42 and W43)

PHASE 11 (More Bugs Found By Actually Using The App -- 2026-07-19 addition, found via W44's launch_sandbox.py)
  W05 (completed) → W45 (root cause: /api/fixtures never sources FINISHED results, so any past-dated window -- a historical sandbox date, or real off-season -- returns nothing regardless of real data availability; breaks Dashboard, Match Explorer search, and the manual-bet-log search bar identically)
  W45 → W46 (empty-window fallback to the next N upcoming/nearest matches -- independently useful today for real off-season, but only fully correct for a sandbox historical date once W45 also lands)
  W11 (completed) → W47 (frontend never calls the already-existing cache-read endpoint -- every card expansion/analysis page load live-generates instead of reading a precomputed recommendation; independent of W45/W46)
  W45 + W46 (both completed) → W48 (found immediately after W45/W46 shipped: real FINISHED status leaks through for fixtures still "in the future" relative to sandbox asOf, defeating the sandbox's no-lookahead purpose on the Dashboard fallback and Match Explorer)
  W07 + W09 + W28 (all completed) → W49 (root cause: the manual "regenerate now" path never fetches odds the way the scheduled EOD/T-30 pipeline does, causing avoidable insufficient_data results even when real odds exist -- not sandbox-exclusive)
  W44 + W49 → W50 (pre-compute recommendations at sandbox launch time, mirroring the live EOD batch -- only worth building once W49's odds fix makes the precomputed results good, not just fast)
  W46 + W50 → W51 (found live: --precompute only covers the exact SANDBOX_DATE, not the 90-day fallback window W46 shows when that date is empty -- most of what a user actually sees was never precomputed)

  W47 + W50 (both completed) → W53 (root cause: the initial fixture list is built by fixtureToMatch(), which always hardcodes hasRecommendation: false and never consults the cache -- W47's cache-read and W50/W51's precompute both only become visible on a per-card click, so a "fully precomputed" scenario still shows every card as "Not yet generated" until individually expanded)
  W51 + W53 (both completed) → W54 (found while investigating many poor/insufficient sandbox recommendations for `python scripts/launch_sandbox.py 2026-03-08 --precompute`: fallback-window fixtures are generated with the sandbox as-of date instead of each fixture's actual date, so fresh precompute rows are stored under the wrong cache key and historical odds lookup returns no odds for those later fixtures)

PHASE 20 (Edge Display & Ranking Correctness -- 2026-08-08 addition)
  -- Both found live via direct user report, both root-caused to the same helper: bestMarket() picked purely by highest value_edge, with no regard for whether that market was actually being recommended. --
  W90 (bestMarket() prefers an actionable direct_bet/conditional market over a numerically higher-edge no_bet one; a shown no_bet market's edge no longer renders in the "good"/green color)
  W90 → W91 (rankTopEdges/sortMatches share bestMarket()'s pricedEdge() helper -- fixing W90 alone left every no_bet match tied at the same excluded rank, so "Edge %" sort no longer ordered them by their own displayed number; needed a two-tier sort, not just the shared helper's own fix)
  W92 (independent -- direct user request, "Today's Edges" renamed "Daily Edges" now that the Dashboard always spans multiple days, not just today)

PHASE 21 (Scheduler Coexistence Under Real Concurrent Operation -- 2026-08-08 addition)
  -- Motivated directly by Phase 23's own conclusion (documents/user_stories.md): the ML-engine's data refresh is now proven idempotent/recoverable/drift-aware in isolation, but it and the app's own EOD/T-30 recommendation scheduler are two separate, unconnected processes reading/writing the same DuckDB file (data/fpai_core.db) with nothing testing what happens when both are genuinely running at once. Confirmed empirically first (not assumed): DuckDB enforces a real exclusive file lock -- a second process opening *any* connection (even read-only) while a read-write connection is open elsewhere fails immediately with duckdb.IOException, it does not block/wait and does not silently corrupt data. --
  W93 (found live: the manual "regenerate now" endpoint, POST /api/recommendations, has zero handling for this -- an unhandled duckdb.IOException would surface as a raw 500 to the user, unlike every other transient-external-condition path in this app)
  W94 (confirm, don't just assume: the scheduled EOD batch's own already-broad exception handling (eod_batch.py's `except Exception`) incidentally already degrades this same scenario gracefully -- a direct regression test, not a code change)
  W95 (future, a genuine design question not yet decided: should `run_refresh_data` itself retry-with-backoff on a transient lock conflict specifically, rather than its current log-and-reraise-wait-for-next-scheduled-fire behavior, which could silently skip an entire week's refresh on unlucky timing)
```

**Size key:** XS < 2 hrs · S ≈ half day · M ≈ 1 day · L ≈ 2–3 days

| W01 | completed | Scaffold `app/backend` as a FastAPI app that imports `src/agent/graph.run_agent`, `src/agent/agent_config.AgentConfig`, and `src/forecast/forecast_service.ForecastService` directly as Python libraries — no MCP, no subprocess, no HTTP hop to `src/mcp_server.py` (that server is for external third-party agent consumers, not our own first-party app; see Integration Gaps). **Acceptance:** `GET /api/health` returns 200; startup performs a soft check that the configured LLM provider (Ollama daemon or Anthropic key) is reachable and logs a warning (not a hard failure) if not. | Size S · Milestone M1 · Depends on: none. **Completion notes (2026-07-11):** `app/backend/main.py` — FastAPI app with a `lifespan` context manager (not the deprecated `on_event("startup")`) running `app/backend/llm_check.check_llm_reachable()` at boot; `GET /api/health` returns `{"status": "ok"}`. `check_llm_reachable()`: for `provider: ollama`, a real probe against the local daemon's `/api/tags` (2s timeout, any exception → unreachable, never raises); for `provider: anthropic`, presence of `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` only — a live call would spend real API credits just to check connectivity, not worth it for a soft startup check, and mirrors how `TAVILY_API_KEY` is already handled elsewhere in this codebase (presence-check, not a live call). `fastapi`/`uvicorn`/`httpx` added to `requirements.txt` (were already present transitively via other packages, now pinned as real first-party deps). Doesn't yet import `run_agent`/`ForecastService` directly — those land with W02, the first endpoint that actually needs them; W01's own scope is the scaffold + health + startup check only. TDD: `app/backend/tests/test_health.py` + `test_llm_check.py` (7 tests, Ollama mocked via `unittest.mock.patch` so the suite doesn't depend on a real daemon running) all failed correctly (`ModuleNotFoundError`) before the files existed. Verified live: booted the real server with `uvicorn`, confirmed `GET /api/health` returns 200 and no startup warning was logged (Ollama is genuinely running in this environment). Full suite (`tests/` + `app/backend/tests/`): 312 passed/1 skipped, zero regressions. |
| W02 | completed | `POST /api/recommendations` — accepts `{home_team, away_team, date, league?, odds?}`, calls `run_agent()` off the event loop (`run_in_threadpool`/`asyncio.to_thread`, since it's a synchronous 10-30s call), returns the `MatchRecommendation` JSON. Adds a Pydantic validation layer independent of the agent's own `extract_recommendation` (which only checks key presence, not value types — see Gaps) with graceful degradation: a malformed market is flagged/hidden, not a 500 for the whole request. **Acceptance:** valid request against a known E0 fixture returns a schema-valid `MatchRecommendation`; a mocked malformed market (e.g. `value_edge` as a string) degrades gracefully instead of crashing the endpoint. | Size M · Milestone M1 · Depends on: W01. **Completion notes (2026-07-11):** `app/backend/recommendations.py` — `RecommendationRequest` (Pydantic, maps directly to `run_agent()`'s `match_info` shape via `.to_match_info()`) and `validate_and_degrade()`, a genuinely independent Pydantic layer (its own `MarketRecommendationOut`/`MatchRecommendationOut` models, not reused from `src.agent.schema`) that drops any market failing validation rather than raising, appending a countable `invalid_market_count` and a limitations note — deliberately still built even though A28 already added strong validation agent-side, since the app should never assume a recommendation it receives (now or from a future cache predating a fix) is trustworthy. Wired into `main.py`'s new `POST /api/recommendations` via `starlette.concurrency.run_in_threadpool`. TDD: `test_recommendations_schema.py` (4 tests, pure validation logic) + `test_recommendations_endpoint.py` (4 tests, full `TestClient` requests with `run_agent` mocked at the app boundary) all failed correctly before the code existed. Live call against `GET /v4/sports/soccer_epl/odds` (1 of 500 monthly credits used) returned 10 real events; `_normalize()` needed **no changes** — real bookmaker/market/outcome structure, decimal prices, and the literal `"Draw"` outcome name all matched the publicly-documented schema it was built against. Added `test_normalize_matches_a_real_captured_response` (`app/backend/tests/test_odds_api_client.py`) using the real captured event (trimmed to 2 of 19 bookmakers) as a permanent regression fixture; removed the "not live-verified" caveat from the module docstring. **Two things the live check did surface, tracked as known limitations rather than fixed here** (independent of `_normalize()`'s own correctness, not in this story's scope): (1) `bookmakers[]` ordering is confirmed *not* stable across events (9/10 had `betfair_sb_uk` first, 1/10 had `betfred_uk` first) — `_normalize()` only ever reads `bookmakers[0]`, no fallback if it lacks the `h2h` market; (2) real responses carry authoritative credit-usage headers (`x-requests-remaining`/`x-requests-used`) that `CreditCounter` never reads, relying only on a client-side estimate that happened to agree this time. **Also surfaced a real, separate bug** in the same live check — cross-referencing these 10 real events against real football-data.org fixtures found 6/10 (60%) with differently-spelled team names between the two providers, meaning `eod_batch.py`'s exact-string-equality odds-to-fixture matching would silently drop real odds for most real fixtures. Logged and fixed as **BUG-015** (see `bugs.md`) — `odds_lookup()`/`match_odds()` now map both sides through the same `TeamNameMapper`/`config/team_mapping.json` ingestion already uses; re-verified 10/10 real fixtures resolve correctly against the fixed code. |
| W08 | completed | Scheduler infrastructure — an in-process scheduler (e.g. APScheduler) running alongside FastAPI, timezone-safe via `zoneinfo("America/New_York")` (correctly handles EST/EDT, not a fixed UTC offset). Job state must be re-derivable from persisted fixture/kickoff data on startup, so a backend restart doesn't silently drop a pending T-30 job or miss the daily EOD window without catching up. Built last, right before going live — see the sequencing note above. **Acceptance:** scheduler survives a process restart without losing track of which jobs are still due; a unit test simulates a restart mid-day and confirms missed jobs are detected and run, not skipped. | Size M · Milestone M7 (built last) · Depends on: none. **Completion notes (2026-07-12):** `app/backend/scheduler.py` (new) — `JobRunLog` (SQLite, `app/data/job_runs.db`, same pattern as W11/W12's stores): persists `(job_id, run_key)` "already ran" markers, keyed by date-isoformat for daily jobs and a fixed `"once"` sentinel for one-off jobs. `RecoverableScheduler` wraps APScheduler's `BackgroundScheduler(timezone=NY_TZ)` and deliberately does **not** rely on APScheduler's own (in-memory) jobstore surviving a restart — every `schedule_daily()`/`schedule_once()` call both registers the normal future trigger *and* immediately checks `JobRunLog`: if the trigger time has already passed and the job hasn't been marked as run, it runs immediately as a catch-up, exactly once (a follow-up registration after catch-up does not re-run it). This generic primitive is what W09 (daily EOD job) and W10 (per-fixture one-off T-30 jobs) both build on. **Acceptance verified directly:** `test_restart_mid_day_detects_and_runs_a_missed_job_only_once` explicitly simulates two separate process instances sharing the same on-disk `JobRunLog` — instance A registers before the trigger time (no-op), instance B registers after it (catches up, runs once), instance C registers again after that (does not double-run). **Hardened while wiring W09/W10 on top:** `_run_and_mark` now catches and logs any exception from the job body rather than letting it propagate — `schedule_daily()`/`schedule_once()`'s catch-up path runs synchronously in the *caller's* thread (unlike APScheduler's own later trigger fires, which run on its own background thread and are already exception-isolated there), so an unguarded failure would otherwise crash whoever is registering the job — at worst, the whole app's startup, if a real network error happened to occur during an immediate catch-up. A failed run is also not marked as done, so the next registration retries it. TDD: `app/backend/tests/test_scheduler.py` (10 tests — `JobRunLog` persistence, daily/once catch-up when time has passed, no-op when not yet due, no double-run once marked, the explicit restart narrative, NY-timezone confirmation, and the exception-safety fix). Full suite: 465 passed/1 skipped, zero regressions. |
| W09 | completed | End-of-day scheduled batch generation (D2a) — fires once daily at end-of-day America/New_York time (exact trigger TBD, proposed 23:00). Fetches the next day's E0 fixtures (W05), current odds (W07), and generates recommendations concurrently using the fault-tolerant pattern from `_run_backtest_concurrent()` (agent_techspec.md §13 — bounded `asyncio.Semaphore`, `asyncio.to_thread` per match, skip-and-continue on error), writing each into W11's cache. Also responsible for scheduling each fixture's individual W10 job. Built last, right before going live — see the sequencing note above; until then, recommendations are generated via W11's manual "regenerate now" path. **Acceptance:** a simulated EOD run for a day with N E0 fixtures produces N cached recommendations (or fewer, with a logged skip count, if one match errors) and N scheduled T-30 jobs. | Size M · Milestone M7 (built last) · Depends on: W02, W05, W06, W07, W08, W11. **Completion notes (2026-07-12):** `app/backend/eod_batch.py` (new) — `run_eod_batch()` mirrors `_run_backtest_concurrent()`'s exact shape: bounded `asyncio.Semaphore`, `asyncio.to_thread` per match (`recommendations.run_agent`, module-qualified for patchability), per-match try/except that logs and increments a skip count rather than aborting the whole batch. Odds are matched to fixtures by exact `(home_team, away_team)` string equality (case-insensitive) — The Odds API and football-data.org share no common ID, and no live Odds API key was available at W07's implementation time to verify their real naming conventions agree, so a fixture with no matching odds event proceeds with odds omitted rather than blocking the batch (same for W07's own credit-budget-exhausted `None` return, and for no odds client configured at all). `schedule_t30` (the W10 hook) is called once per fixture unconditionally, regardless of whether EOD generation succeeded for that particular match — W10's own refresh is independently best-effort and shouldn't depend on tonight's batch having gone cleanly. TDD: `app/backend/tests/test_eod_batch.py` (6 tests). Also built the W08→W09→W10 wiring (`app/backend/scheduler_wiring.py`): `next_day_date_str()` (NY-timezone tomorrow), `PersistingOddsClient` (W07's `OddsAPIClient`/`CreditCounter`/`FileCreditCounterStore` trio deliberately leaves credit-counter persistence to the caller — this is that caller), `register_eod_job()` (registers the daily job on a `RecoverableScheduler`). Wired into `app/backend/main.py`'s `lifespan` — **gated behind `ENABLE_SCHEDULER=1` (off by default)**: registering the job runs W08's restart/catch-up check *immediately*, so wiring it in unconditionally would make every test file's `with TestClient(app)` non-deterministically trigger a real live EOD batch (real fixtures/odds/LLM calls) depending on whatever wall-clock time tests happen to run at relative to the 23:00 NY trigger — confirmed this risk is real by checking real NY time during live verification. This is a deliberate engineering call, not requested in the story text; flagging it explicitly since it's the difference between "built" and "actually turned on." TDD: `app/backend/tests/test_scheduler_wiring.py` (6 tests, including one end-to-end wiring check with a simulated past-due 'now' confirming both the EOD job and its T-30 follow-up fire and get marked as run). Full suite: 465 passed/1 skipped, zero regressions. **Verified live:** booted the real FastAPI app once with `ENABLE_SCHEDULER` unset (confirmed scheduler stays off, logs the disabled message) and once with `ENABLE_SCHEDULER=1` set (confirmed it registers without crashing; real NY time at check-time was before today's 23:00 trigger, so no live catch-up fired during the check, as expected). |
| W10 | completed | Per-match refresh 30 minutes before kickoff (D2a) — fetches fresh odds from W07 first and compares them against the odds used for the currently-cached recommendation (stored by W11). **Only re-runs `run_agent()` if the odds actually changed** (exact match = skip, logged as "no new data, refresh skipped") — this both saves LLM/Tavily cost and reduces how often the agent's known run-to-run non-reproducibility (agent_techspec.md §18.6) gets exercised for no reason. If odds did change, re-runs and overwrites the cached recommendation. Best-effort: if W07's credit budget is exhausted or the fixture was removed/postponed, skips gracefully rather than erroring. Built last, right before going live — see the sequencing note above. **Acceptance:** a fixture whose odds haven't moved since W09's generation shows zero new `run_agent()` calls at its T-30 window in logs; a fixture whose odds did move gets a measurably updated cached recommendation (new `generated_at`); a simulated exhausted-budget case leaves the prior recommendation in place rather than failing. | Size M · Milestone M7 (built last) · Depends on: W07, W09, W11. **Completion notes (2026-07-12):** `app/backend/t30_refresh.py` (new) — `refresh_match_at_t30()`: fetches fresh odds, matches to the fixture by team name (shared helpers `match_odds()`/`odds_lookup()` promoted to public in `eod_batch.py` so both modules use the identical matching logic), compares against `cache.get_latest(...)`'s stored odds dict for exact equality. Equal → `skipped_no_change`, logged "no new data, refresh skipped," zero new cache rows or `run_agent` calls (verified via `cache.get_history()` length staying at 1). Different, or no prior cache entry at all (e.g. tonight's EOD generation errored for this match — nothing to lose by generating fresh) → calls `run_agent`, writes a new cache row via `record_generation(triggered_by="scheduled")` (`TriggeredBy` already only has two values, `scheduled`/`manual_regenerate` — no schema change needed; W09 and W10 are both "scheduled," distinguished by the odds/timestamp history, not by a separate tag). No odds returned at all (W07's credit-budget-exhausted `None`, no odds client configured, or the fixture simply not found in the current odds feed — e.g. postponed) → `skipped_no_odds`, prior recommendation left untouched. A `run_agent` exception → `skipped_error`, prior recommendation also left untouched. **Deliberately synchronous** (not `async def`) — a single match has no internal concurrency to bound, and making it async while called from within W09's own async `schedule_t30` catch-up path caused a real `RuntimeError: asyncio.run() cannot be called from a running event loop` the first time it was wired end-to-end (caught via the wiring test, fixed by dropping `async`, not by adding a workaround). TDD: `app/backend/tests/test_t30_refresh.py` (7 tests). Full suite (with W08/W09): 465 passed/1 skipped, zero regressions. |
| W11 | completed | Recommendation caching layer in `app/backend` — built before W09/W10 since both write into it. **Not** a reuse of `SnapshotStore` (that component's record/replay semantics are purpose-built for backtest determinism, keyed by tool-call SHA-256, gitignored — repurposing it for live caching would conflate two different concerns). New table/store keyed by `(match_id, date, agent_config_hash)`; the dashboard/API never calls `run_agent()` synchronously in the normal path. Each cache row stores the odds it was generated against (not just the recommendation itself), so W10 can cheaply detect "no new data" before deciding whether to regenerate — a lightweight generation history (timestamp + odds snapshot per generation, not just the latest) is worth keeping for the same reason and doubles as an audit trail. A manual "regenerate now" escape hatch remains for dev/testing and late-added fixtures. **Acceptance:** `/api/recommendations` reads exclusively from this cache for scheduled fixtures; each cache entry exposes the odds it was generated from; the manual regenerate path is a distinct, explicitly-triggered action. | Size S · Milestone M2 · Depends on: W02. **Completion notes (2026-07-11):** `app/backend/recommendation_cache.py` — SQLite-backed (`app/data/recommendation_cache.db`, gitignored; a genuinely new store, no `SnapshotStore` reuse), single append-only `recommendation_generations` table keyed by `(match_id, date, agent_config_hash)` — every generation is kept as its own row (`get_history`), not just the latest (`get_latest`), doubling as the audit trail the story asks for. `app/backend/agent_config_hash.py::compute_agent_config_hash()` hashes the tunable `AgentConfig` fields so a config change naturally produces a new cache key. Split `/api/recommendations` into two distinct routes per the acceptance: **`POST /api/recommendations`** (existing from W02) is now explicitly the "regenerate now" escape hatch — still always calls `run_agent()`, but also writes the result into the cache tagged `triggered_by="manual_regenerate"`. **`GET /api/recommendations/{match_id}?date=...`** is new and reads *exclusively* from the cache — never calls `run_agent()` — returning 404 if nothing's cached yet for that key. `RecommendationRequest` gained an optional `match_id` (derived from `home_team__away_team__date` if omitted, since W09 — the real fixture-ID source — doesn't exist yet). Cache access is wired via FastAPI's `Depends(recommendations.get_cache)`, overridable in tests. TDD: `test_recommendation_cache.py` (8 tests, pure cache/hash logic) + `test_recommendation_cache_endpoints.py` (5 tests, full `TestClient` round-trips including "GET never calls run_agent") all failed correctly before the code existed. Full suite: 367 passed/1 skipped, zero regressions. Verified live: the real (non-test) default cache path (`app/data/recommendation_cache.db`) initializes and round-trips correctly outside the test harness, and is correctly excluded by `.gitignore` (added `app/data/`, since the existing `data/*.db` rule only matches the top-level engine `data/` dir, not `app/data/`). |
| W12 | completed | `user_bets` table + CRUD endpoints (D3a). Schema: `date`, `home_team`, `away_team`, `market`, `selection`, `odds`, `outcome` (`open`\|`won`\|`lost`), `stake`, `profit_loss` (computed: `stake × (odds − 1)` if won, `−stake` if lost, `null` while open). Two logging paths: (1) from a recommendation — every field auto-filled and locked from the selected recommendation except `stake`, the only user input, with the full `MatchRecommendation` snapshotted alongside (recommendations aren't reproducible run-to-run — agent_techspec.md §18.6); (2) manual — user selects a real fixture via the Match Explorer's existing search (not freeform team names, so auto-settlement still works) and fills in market/selection/odds/stake themselves, no snapshot. **Acceptance:** both logging paths produce a row with all required fields; the recommendation path rejects an attempt to edit any field but stake; the manual path requires a resolved fixture reference, not free text. | Size M · Milestone M3 · Depends on: W04, W11. **Completion notes (2026-07-11):** `app/backend/bet_tracker.py` — SQLite-backed (`app/data/user_bets.db`), `settle_bet()` computes `profit_loss` per the exact formula (W13 will call this). `app/backend/bets.py` — `BetFromRecommendationRequest` has **no fields at all** for odds/home_team/away_team/date; they're always derived from the recommendation snapshot's own matching market (`resolve_from_recommendation()`), so there's structurally nothing for a client to submit except `stake`, `market`/`selection` (which entry to bet on), and the snapshot itself — verified via a test asserting the exact field set, and an endpoint test confirming a client-supplied `odds`/`home_team` in the request body is silently ignored. `BetManualRequest.match_id` requires non-empty (422 otherwise). Routes: `POST /api/bets/from-recommendation`, `POST /api/bets/manual`, `GET /api/bets`. **Frontend** (not explicitly required by the acceptance criteria, but the story clearly describes real UI interactions — built to match W04/W15/W16's precedent): `MatchAnalysisPage` gained an inline `LogBetButton` per market row (stake input + confirm, using the already-fetched recommendation as the locked snapshot); new `/bets` page (`components/BetTracker.tsx`) lists logged bets and provides the manual-entry form (search real fixtures via `/api/fixtures`, select one, fill market/selection/odds/stake). | W13 | completed | Auto-settlement job. Sourced from **on-demand API calls (W05's football-data.org results client), not a query against the DuckDB `raw_matches` table** — `raw_matches` is a batch-refreshed table (currently stale since 2026-05-24, see Operational gaps) and structurally unsuitable for near-real-time settlement of a match that just finished. Only `_market_correct()`'s pure resolution logic is reused from `src/agent/backtest.py` (extracted into a shared utility) — `load_outcome()` itself is *not* reused as-is, since it derives its `actual` dict from a DataFrame row sourced from DuckDB; the settlement job instead builds that same `actual` dict shape from W05's live API result. Covers `result_3way`/`btts`/`total_goals`. **Corners markets are excluded from auto-settlement, accepted as an ongoing limitation, not just a v1 gap** (see Integration Gaps — no numeric line field, not fixable app-side) and stay `open` pending manual settlement. **Acceptance:** a settled E0 fixture, checked via a live W05 API call rather than any local database query, auto-updates any open bet on a scorable market to `won`/`lost` and computes `profit_loss`; a corners bet is never auto-settled, silently or otherwise. | Size M · Milestone M3 · Depends on: W05, W12. **Completion notes (2026-07-12):** `src/agent/market_resolution.py` (new) — extracted `RESOLVABLE_MARKETS`/`market_correct()` out of `src/agent/backtest.py` (which now imports them instead of maintaining its own copy) plus a new `build_actual_outcome(home_goals, away_goals)` helper usable from any live-result source, not just a DataFrame row; `backtest.py`'s `load_outcome()` is now a one-line wrapper around it. `app/backend/settlement.py` (new) — `settle_open_bets(tracker, client)`: filters `list_open_bets()` down to `RESOLVABLE_MARKETS` only (a corners bet is filtered out before any API call is even made), groups the remaining bets by `date` to make one `get_results()` call per distinct date rather than per bet (respects W05's ~10-req/min budget), builds the `actual` outcome from each matching `NormalizedMatch`'s goals via `build_actual_outcome()`, and calls `BetTracker.settle_bet()` for anything `market_correct()` resolves to non-`None`. Endpoint: `POST /api/bets/settle-open` (on-demand, no scheduler — W08/W09 are deliberately deferred), reusing `get_fixtures_client()` rather than a second client instance. **Frontend:** `BetTrackerPage` gained a "Settle open bets" button showing a result message ("Settled N bet(s)." / "No open bets have a finished, scorable result yet.") and reloading the bet list. | W14 | completed | Bet tracker summary stats (ROI, hit rate, running bankroll), adapting `src/agent/evaluation.py`'s report-building logic (`compute_max_drawdown`, etc.) for a running single-user ledger instead of a fixed backtest batch. **Acceptance:** the bet tracker page shows ROI/hit-rate/bankroll-curve computed only over settled (`won`/`lost`) bets, recalculated as new bets settle. | Size M · Milestone M3 · Depends on: W13. **Completion notes (2026-07-12):** `app/backend/bet_stats.py` (new) — `compute_bet_stats(bets, starting_bankroll=1000.0)` reuses `src/agent/evaluation.py`'s `compute_max_drawdown()` verbatim against a notional equity curve built from settled bets' already-computed `profit_loss` (in `list_bets()`'s id/creation order); `build_evaluation_report()` itself was *not* reused as-is since it's shaped around backtest's `BankrollResult`/`bet.payout`/`bet.won`, not the `Bet` dataclass — the ROI/hit-rate arithmetic is the same formula, recomputed for the different data shape. `starting_bankroll` default (1000.0) matches the existing convention in `src/agent/staking.py`'s `simulate_flat_stake`/`simulate_kelly_stake` — a notional bankroll for computing ROI/drawdown against, not a claim about real funds. | W27 | completed | **Sandbox clock — an app-wide, overridable "as-of now."** Off-season + no live-data volume (2026-07 discussion) means testing whole user journeys needs to pretend "today" is a chosen historical date instead of the real wall clock. A single shared function (e.g. `is_sandbox_mode()`/`sandbox_now()` in a new `app/backend/sandbox_clock.py`) is driven by two env vars, `SANDBOX_MODE=1` and `SANDBOX_DATE=YYYY-MM-DD` — both absent by default, so normal operation is completely unaffected (purely additive, not a new mode every code path must branch on defensively). Every backend call site that currently computes "today"/"now" directly for date-window purposes (the Dashboard's default fixture query, W09's `next_day_date_str`, the status footer's staleness calc, etc.) is routed through this instead of a bare `datetime.now()`. New `GET /api/sandbox/status` returns `{"sandbox_mode": bool, "as_of": "YYYY-MM-DD" \| null}` so both the frontend (W30) and test scripts (W31) can introspect the active sandbox date rather than each needing their own access to the env vars. **Not a literal container** (Docker etc.) — "sandbox" here means an isolated *configuration and data* mode within the existing app, per the 2026-07-16 conceptual-only decision. **Acceptance:** with `SANDBOX_MODE` unset, every existing test and behavior is byte-for-byte unchanged; with `SANDBOX_MODE=1`/`SANDBOX_DATE=<a real historical date>` set, `GET /api/sandbox/status` reflects it, and at least one existing "today"-dependent code path demonstrably uses that date instead of the real one. | Size S · Milestone M6 · Depends on: none (foundational, independent). **Completion notes (2026-07-17):** `app/backend/sandbox_clock.py` — `is_sandbox_mode()`, `sandbox_date()`, `sandbox_now(tz)`, `sandbox_status()`, plus a `sandbox_scoped_path(filename)` helper (added during W29 review after the exact `app/backend/` vs `app/` parent-depth arithmetic it centralizes caused a real bug there — see W29's notes). `GET /api/sandbox/status` added to `main.py`; `scheduler_wiring.py`'s `next_day_date_str()`/`register_eod_job()` default clock routed through `sandbox_now(NY_TZ)`. AS DOUBLE), 2)` (lossless, since football-data.co.uk odds are always quoted to 2dp; confirmed against the real production schema in `src/ingestion/football_data/loader.py`, not just the test's synthetic table). 5 tests including an end-to-end check through the real, unmodified `odds_lookup()`/`match_odds()` (BUG-015). | W30 | completed | **Frontend sandbox-clock awareness.** Dashboard, Match Explorer, and the status footer currently anchor their date-window logic to the browser's own `new Date()`. Each now fetches W27's `GET /api/sandbox/status` once and uses its `as_of` date as "today" instead, whenever `sandbox_mode` is true — with zero behavior change when it's false (the existing, real-`Date()` code path is untouched). This must work for **both a human clicking through the real UI** as if it were a chosen historical date, **and Playwright-driven automated checks** — both explicitly required, not just one. **Acceptance:** with the backend in sandbox mode for a real historical date with real E0 fixtures (needs W29 to verify live, though the frontend change itself only needs W27's endpoint to exist), the Dashboard shows that date's real fixtures instead of today's real, likely off-season-empty list, with the only date-logic change being `as_of` swapped in for `new Date()`. | Size S · Milestone M6 · Depends on: W27; verified live end-to-end via W29. **Completion notes (2026-07-17):** `app/frontend/lib/useSandboxAsOf.ts` — fetches `GET /api/sandbox/status` once per mount, returns `new Date()` unless sandbox mode is active. Wired into Dashboard's fixture query and Match Explorer's 90-day window (`MatchUI.tsx`) and `ManualBetForm`'s fixture search (`BetTracker.tsx`); `StatusFooter.tsx` needed no change since its staleness display is entirely server-computed (no frontend `new Date()` call of its own), narrowing the acceptance text above to what's actually true. Review caught and fixed two real, symmetric timezone bugs before merge: (1) the hook originally constructed `as_of` via `new Date(`${as_of}T00:00:00`)` (local midnight), but every consumer re-serializes via `.toISOString()` (UTC) — in any positive-UTC-offset timezone (most of Asia/Australia) this silently showed the *previous* day's fixtures, defeating the story's entire purpose; fixed to construct UTC midnight directly (`new Date(as_of)`, correct per ECMA-262 for a bare date string). (2) That fix then exposed the exact inverse in the 90-day window's `to.setDate(to.getDate() + 90)`, which read the now-UTC `asOf` via local getters — truncating the window by a day in negative-UTC-offset zones; fixed to `setUTCDate`/`getUTCDate`. Both verified independently across 5 timezones (UTC, Asia/Tokyo, America/Los_Angeles, Pacific/Kiritimati, Pacific/Midway) with identical, correct results. 20/20 frontend tests, `tsc --noEmit` clean. Commits `a0474ac`, `9c2be2a`, `6a58596`. **Follow-up from the final whole-branch review:** `MatchCard`'s `formatDay()` (the "today"/"tomorrow" label shown on every fixture card before a recommendation exists) had been missed by both this story and W38 — it still called `new Date()` directly. Fixing it (commit `94167c1`) via uniform UTC getters on `asOf` introduced a *third*, fourth-round timezone bug: real (non-sandbox) users would see the wrong relative-day label for roughly half of every day in any non-UTC timezone, since a real `new Date()` needs local getters, not UTC ones — `asOf`'s correct getter frame depends on whether it's sandbox-encoded or real, which can't be inferred from the Date object alone. Fixed properly in `dcf3d0b`: `useSandboxAsOf()` now returns `{ asOf, sandboxMode }` instead of a bare `Date`, and `formatDay()` branches explicitly on `sandboxMode` (UTC getters only when true). Verified byte-identical to the pre-branch original for the real-clock case, across the same 5 timezones plus a dedicated regression test proving time-of-day independence. Also surfaced, but left out of scope as genuinely pre-existing (present since `bf4277c`, before this branch): `MatchExplorerPage`/`ManualBetForm`'s 90-day fixture-search window slices a real `asOf` instant via `.toISOString()`, which can shift the search start by a day near a viewer's local-midnight boundary in non-UTC timezones — not introduced or worsened by this branch, worth its own future story if it matters in practice. 26/26 frontend tests, `tsc --noEmit` clean. |

| W37 | completed | **Wire `SnapshotStore` record/replay into the sandbox agent-invocation path (the "web-search leakage" fix, item C).** The app's real agent-invocation path (`recommendations.run_agent`) never calls `configure_snapshot_store()` at all, so `SnapshotStore.mode` always stays at its default, `"live"` — the one mode `src/agent/tools.py::web_search`'s `before:<match_date>` leakage filter does *not* cover (it already covers `"record"` **and** `"replay"`, per A10/BUG-011 — this needs no change to `tools.py` itself). This matters once sandbox mode (W27) makes the agent reason about a chosen past date while the real world has moved on: an unfiltered `web_search` could return real, current results — or worse, the sandboxed match's own real final score, since the *only* safeguard against that today is a soft, LLM-obeyed docstring instruction, not a code-level filter. Fix: when sandbox mode is active, the app's agent-invocation path calls `configure_snapshot_store("record", match_id, match_date)` the first time a given sandboxed match is analyzed (a real, bounded, non-deterministic call — same cost/risk profile as any real run, just correctly date-filtered by already-existing infrastructure) and `configure_snapshot_store("replay", match_id, match_date)` on every subsequent run of that same match (zero live calls at all, not just a filtered search — nothing left to leak, and no dependence on Tavily's `before:` filter being a hard guarantee). Recordings live in a sandbox-specific namespace (e.g. `data/agent_snapshots/sandbox/`), kept separate from the real evaluation corpus (A20/A21, the 2026-07-05 collection). **Acceptance:** a sandboxed match's first run makes a real, date-filtered `web_search` call and is recorded; a second run of the identical sandboxed match makes zero live calls (confirmed via a network-call assertion, same technique as W20's guard) and returns the identical recorded recommendation. | Size M · Milestone M6 · Depends on: W27, W29 (completed). **Completion notes (2026-07-17):** `src/agent/tools.py`'s `configure_snapshot_store()` gained an optional `base_dir` param; `app/backend/recommendations.py`'s `run_agent()` wraps the real agent call — sandbox mode records the first run of a given match (date-filtered `web_search`) and replays every subsequent run (zero live calls) in a separate `data/agent_snapshots/sandbox/` namespace. TDD against the real, pre-existing agent test suite (not just new tests) caught two real bugs before merge: (1) the plan's literal "reset `base_dir` to default when omitted" logic would have broken `tests/test_agent_tools_snapshot.py`'s existing `autouse` fixture (which sets `base_dir` directly and expects bare mode-only calls to stick) and leaked real snapshot files to disk during ordinary test runs — fixed by making `base_dir` sticky-if-omitted, mirroring `match_id`'s existing convention; (2) review then found a genuine thread-safety race in that same `base_dir` swap (a plain global reassignment, unprotected, reachable via `run_in_threadpool`-driven concurrent `/api/recommendations` requests) that could silently drop a sandboxed request back into unfiltered `"live"` mode — fixed with a `threading.Lock` scoped tightly around the fast configure step only (not the slow LLM call, so no serialization of concurrent requests). Also closed a TOCTOU race on the record/replay decision itself, unified duplicated match-key construction with `effective_match_id()`, and added a test for the `try/finally` exception path. 5 tests; full suite 162 backend / 479 passed combined; backward-compat trio (`test_agent_tools_snapshot.py`/`test_snapshot_store.py`/`test_backtest.py`) 24/24, zero snapshot leakage to the real `data/agent_snapshots/` corpus. Commits `85d9bfd`, `62e486b`. |
