| W40 | active | **Standardize frontend "now" to America/New_York (Eastern Time), not the browser's ambient timezone (2026-07-18 decision).** The backend has always anchored every time-dependent computation to a single canonical zone — `NY_TZ = ZoneInfo("America/New_York")` drives the EOD batch trigger, T-30 refresh, and DST-aware scheduling throughout `app/backend/scheduler.py`. The frontend never got the same treatment: `MatchExplorerPage`'s and `ManualBetForm`'s 90-day fixture-search windows (`app/frontend/components/MatchUI.tsx`, `BetTracker.tsx`) and `MatchCard`'s `formatDay()` real-clock fallback (W30/`dcf3d0b`) all still fall back to the browser's own ambient local timezone via a bare `new Date()`, then convert via `.toISOString()`/local Date getters — a real, if narrow, bug: for a viewer in a negative-UTC-offset timezone during the hours after UTC midnight but before their own local midnight, the computed "today" lands one calendar day ahead of what they'd call today, shifting the 90-day window's edges (traced to pre-existing code, commit `bf4277c`, predating W27-W39 — not introduced by any of that work). Since this is a single-user app (`agent_prd.md` §2.1) and the backend is already Eastern-Time-anchored throughout, there's no need for the frontend to handle "the viewer's local timezone" generically — pin production's default "now" to America/New_York everywhere, exactly like the backend does. **This should also let `formatDay()`'s `sandboxMode` branching (added in `dcf3d0b` specifically to pick UTC vs. local Date getters depending on whether `asOf` was sandbox-encoded or a real browser instant) collapse to a single code path** — once the real-clock case is *also* always expressed in one canonical zone rather than the browser's ambient one, there's only ever one reference frame in play, real or sandboxed, removing the need to branch on which getter is correct. Testing overrides continue to use the existing sandbox mechanism (`useSandboxAsOf()`/`SANDBOX_MODE`/`SANDBOX_DATE`, W27) unchanged — no new override mechanism needed, this story only changes what the *non-sandboxed default* resolves to. **Acceptance:** a single shared helper (e.g. `nowInEasternTime()` in `app/frontend/lib/`) replaces every remaining bare `new Date()` used for "today" computation in production code paths; a test suite run under multiple `TZ` env values (at minimum `UTC`, `Asia/Tokyo`, `America/Los_Angeles`) produces identical computed dates/windows, proving the browser's ambient timezone no longer affects the result. | Size S · Milestone M7 · Depends on: none (independently buildable) |
| W41 | active | **Scheduler live-fire smoke test for `CronTrigger` (2026-07-18 addition, follow-up to W33).** W21's and W33's scheduler tests are thorough about `JobRunLog`'s restart/catch-up logic — multi-instance restarts, multi-day soak, DST transitions — but every one of them only ever exercises the *immediate catch-up* path (`RecoverableScheduler.schedule_daily()`/`schedule_once()`'s own `now >= trigger_today` check), never APScheduler's own live `CronTrigger` actually firing on a real background thread during continuous operation. These are genuinely different code paths: catch-up is our own manual datetime comparison, live firing is APScheduler's internal trigger-scheduling machinery — a bug specific to how `CronTrigger` interprets `hour`/`minute`/`timezone` (an APScheduler version quirk, a timezone-object mismatch) would not be caught by anything in this suite today, since catch-up never routes through it. **Accepted trade-off, not fixed here:** `JobRunLog`'s catch-up logic would still recover from a genuinely missed live fire on the next restart — this gap is specifically about verifying the live-fire mechanism exists and works at all, not about restart safety, which is already well covered by W21/W33. **Acceptance:** a new test schedules a `CronTrigger` a couple of real seconds in the future (a genuine, short wall-clock wait — not simulated — marked `@pytest.mark.live` per the existing network-guard opt-out convention in `app/backend/tests/conftest.py`, so it's excluded from the default fast run), starts the scheduler for real, and confirms the job fires via the live trigger path — distinguished from the catch-up path by registering the job with a trigger time still in the future at registration, so the immediate catch-up check cannot be what fired it. | Size XS · Milestone M7 · Depends on: W08 (completed) |
| W42 | completed | **Fix a real fixture-fetch race condition: a stale response can silently overwrite fresh sandbox-correct data (found 2026-07-18 by actually launching the app with `SANDBOX_DATE=2025-03-05`).** `DashboardPage`, `MatchExplorerPage` (`app/frontend/components/MatchUI.tsx`), and `ManualBetForm` (`app/frontend/components/BetTracker.tsx`) each `useEffect(() => { load(); }, [asOf])`-style fetch fixtures whenever `asOf` changes, but none of their `load()` functions guard against a stale response landing after a newer one — unlike `useSandboxAsOf()`'s own effect, which already uses a `cancelled` flag for exactly this reason. Concretely reproduced: `useSandboxAsOf()` resolves `asOf` asynchronously (real browser `new Date()` first, then the corrected sandbox value once `GET /api/sandbox/status` returns), so `load()` fires twice in quick succession — once for the wrong (real-clock) date range, once for the correct (sandbox) one. Captured real network timing via Playwright showed the real-clock request's response landing *after* the correct one (React StrictMode's dev-mode double-effect-invocation made this reliably reproducible, but the underlying race exists independent of StrictMode — ordinary network jitter between two overlapping requests can produce the same outcome in production), so the final rendered page showed 60 of today's real fixtures instead of the correct empty sandbox-date result — confirmed both via captured API response bodies/timestamps and a full-page screenshot. **Acceptance:** all three `load()` call sites gain the same `cancelled`-flag guard pattern `useSandboxAsOf.ts` already uses (checked before every `setState` call derived from the fetch); a new test deliberately resolves an earlier request's promise *after* a later one and asserts the later (correct) result wins, not the stale one — the exact scenario the existing W38 tests didn't catch, since their mocked `getFixtures` calls resolve deterministically/in-order rather than exercising out-of-order resolution. | Size S · Milestone M7 · Depends on: W30, W38 (both completed). **Completion notes (2026-07-18):** Applied the same `cancelled`-flag guard pattern from `useSandboxAsOf.ts` to all three call sites. For `DashboardPage`/`MatchExplorerPage`, `load()` moved from the component body into the `useEffect` itself (it needs to close over the effect-scoped `cancelled` variable) — their retry buttons no longer call `load()` imperatively (which couldn't cancel a stale in-flight request from a prior run); they now bump a `retryTick` state included in the effect's dependency array, so a manual retry goes through the same guarded effect run. `ManualBetForm`'s inline `.then(setFixtures).catch(...)` got the same guard with no structural change needed. Added `MatchUI.race.test.tsx` and `BetTracker.race.test.tsx`, each using a `deferred<T>()` helper to deliberately resolve the earlier (real-clock) request's promise *after* the later (sandbox) one, asserting the final rendered state reflects the correct (sandbox) result, not stale real-clock fixtures. **Verified empirically, not just by inspection:** checked out the pre-fix `MatchUI.tsx` and re-ran the new race tests against it — both failed exactly as expected (stale fixtures visible), confirming the tests genuinely catch the bug rather than passing trivially; restored the fix and re-ran, both passed. Full suite: 6 test files, 29 tests, all passing; `tsc --noEmit` clean. Code-quality review approved with two non-blocking nitpicks (a `deferred()` test helper duplicated verbatim across the two new test files, and inconsistent use of RTL's `act()` plus a fixed-timeout wait in the new tests) — left as-is since this codebase has no shared test-utils convention and the flakiness risk is low (only reachable on the negative/bug-reproducing assertion branch). Commit `eb14c8b`. |
| W43 | completed | **Snapshot replay key-miss from LLM tool-call non-determinism should degrade gracefully, not crash the request with a 500 (found 2026-07-18, same live run as W42).** Calling `POST /api/recommendations` twice in a row for the identical sandboxed match (Nottingham vs Manchester City, 2025-03-08) — the second call meant to replay the first's recording — raised an unhandled `SnapshotMissingError` (`src/agent/snapshot_store.py`) that propagated all the way through `recommendations.run_agent()` to a raw FastAPI 500. Root cause is the already-documented, accepted limitation that LLM output isn't reproducible run-to-run (`agent_techspec.md` §18.6: "snapshot replay key misses from LLM-regenerated tool arguments") — `SnapshotStore.wrap()`'s replay-mode lookup key is a hash of the tool call's exact input arguments (e.g. the `web_search` query text the LLM itself generates), and a second real LLM invocation can phrase that query differently than the first, missing the recorded key. **This isn't a new class of bug** — it's the first time anyone has actually triggered it end-to-end with two real, back-to-back LLM calls against the same sandboxed match; every existing W37 test mocked `_real_run_agent`, so none of them could hit a genuine LLM-phrasing mismatch. The gap is purely in how the app *handles* the already-known-possible miss: today, not at all. Fix: catch `SnapshotMissingError` in `recommendations.py`'s sandbox `run_agent()` wrapper and fall back to a fresh `"record"` pass for that one request (matching this codebase's established "the app should never assume the agent/its own optimizations hold, degrade gracefully instead" philosophy — W02/W15/W16's `validate_and_degrade`) rather than failing the request outright; a second, genuinely different failure must still propagate normally, not be silently swallowed forever. **Acceptance:** a test mocks `_real_run_agent` to raise `SnapshotMissingError` on its first call and succeed on a second, confirms `run_agent()` transparently retries once in record mode and returns the successful result (not a 500); a test confirms a *second* real exception (not a replay-miss) still propagates uncaught, so this fallback doesn't become a silent catch-all. | Size S · Milestone M7 · Depends on: W37 (completed). **Completion notes (2026-07-18):** `app/backend/recommendations.py` gained a `_run_agent_in_mode(mode, match_info, config, match_key)` helper (configures the snapshot store, calls the real agent, always resets to `"live"` mode in a `finally`); `run_agent()`'s sandbox branch now catches `SnapshotMissingError` from a replay-mode call and retries exactly once in `"record"` mode, returning that result on success. Any other exception, or a `SnapshotMissingError` raised while already in record mode, propagates uncaught (no infinite retry, no broad catch-all). Added 3 tests to `app/backend/tests/test_sandbox_agent_snapshot.py`: retry-succeeds-in-record-mode, a genuinely different exception still propagates uncaught, and a record-mode miss doesn't trigger a second retry. **TDD verified**: reverted the fix and re-ran the two spec-required tests against pre-fix code — both failed with the raw `SnapshotMissingError` escaping uncaught, confirming they weren't accidental passes; restored the fix, all 8 tests in the file pass. Full suite: 494 passed, 23 skipped, 1 deselected, zero regressions. Code-quality review flagged one Important gap — the replay-miss retry was entirely silent, unlike this codebase's existing precedent for logging comparable fallback events (`main.py`'s LLM-provider-unreachable warning, `tools.py`'s league-model-absent fallback) — fixed in a follow-up commit adding a `_LOG.warning(...)` line at the retry site plus a docstring on `_run_agent_in_mode`, re-verified against the full suite (494 passed, no regressions). Commits `1ccc11e`, `382e264`. |
| W44 | completed | **Automate the sandbox scenario preflight + drive-and-verify workflow (2026-07-18 addition, prompted by the manual W42/W43 discovery session).** Verifying a sandbox scenario today is a manual, multi-step process a human has to drive by hand: check `raw_matches` for real fixtures on/near the candidate date (and find the nearest real matchday if the exact date is empty), check whether snapshots already exist for those matches, launch the backend and frontend, hit the API/UI to confirm sandbox mode engaged correctly, generate a real recommendation, and check data isolation — exactly the sequence that found W42 and W43 today. W31's `scripts/sandbox_runbook.py` already automates the backend-only "drive it" half (fixtures → recommendation → bet logging → settlement) for a date already known to have real data, but has no "preflight" step (report data availability and existing-snapshot status *before* attempting a live run, including suggesting the nearest real matchday if the requested date is empty) and doesn't drive or verify the frontend at all. **Acceptance:** N/A — deferred, scope when picked up; likely shape is extending `scripts/sandbox_runbook.py` with a `--preflight`/dry-run mode (raw_matches + snapshot-existence report, no live calls) and folding in a Playwright-driven frontend check (screenshot + fixture-list assertion) alongside the existing backend steps, so the whole W42/W43-discovery sequence becomes one repeatable command instead of ad hoc investigation. | Size — · Milestone M7 · Depends on: W31 (completed). **Completion notes (2026-07-19):** Built as a new, separate script — `scripts/launch_sandbox.py` — rather than extending `sandbox_runbook.py` in place, since this covers a genuinely different use case: `sandbox_runbook.py` drives the backend-only pipeline with no server involved, while this script boots real HTTP servers (backend `uvicorn` + frontend `next dev`) for interactive human browsing, which is what was actually requested ("so I can use it on my own"). `python scripts/launch_sandbox.py <date>` runs the preflight (real `raw_matches` fixtures for that date, or the nearest matchday with data if empty; a best-effort count of existing `data/agent_snapshots/sandbox/` directories for that date — explicitly documented as approximate, since snapshot directory names are keyed on whatever team-name spelling the live caller used, which doesn't reliably match `raw_matches`'s own Football-Data CSV spelling, e.g. `"Nott'm Forest"` vs `"Nottingham Forest"` vs `"Nottingham"` — all three exist as real snapshot-directory prefixes for the same team in this repo's corpus), a non-blocking environment check (Ollama reachability, `FOOTBALL_DATA_API_KEY`/`TAVILY_API_KEY` presence), then launches both servers as separate process groups, polls each for health, confirms `/api/sandbox/status` reports the requested date, writes a PID/port state file, and prints the URLs. `--dry-run` runs the preflight only. `--stop` reads the state file and `SIGTERM`s both recorded process groups cleanly (verified this avoids the orphaned-child problem a plain `pkill`/parent-PID kill has with `npm run dev` spawning `next dev` as a child). A same-instance-already-running guard prevents a second launch from silently double-starting. **Not included** (out of scope for what was actually asked): the "likely shape" sketch above also proposed folding a Playwright-driven frontend check (screenshot + fixture-list assertion) into the same command — that would automate *verifying* what renders, whereas this script automates *launching* so a human can verify by looking; the two are different requests, and only the latter was made. TDD: `scripts/test_launch_sandbox.py` (17 tests) covers the deterministic, non-networked logic — preflight DB queries against a temp DuckDB (exact-date match, nearest-matchday suggestion, no-data case), state-file read/write/clear, `--stop`'s process-group teardown including the already-gone-process case, and argument parsing — matching the precedent `sandbox_runbook.py`'s own test file already established (only the argument-guard is unit-tested there; real server launches are verified live, not simulated). **Verified live**: ran the full real lifecycle against `SANDBOX_DATE=2025-03-08` — preflight correctly listed the 6 real fixtures for that date; backend and frontend both launched and passed their health checks; `/api/sandbox/status` confirmed `as_of: "2025-03-08"`; a second launch attempt while one was already running was correctly rejected; `--stop` cleanly terminated both process groups (confirmed via `ps aux`, zero orphaned processes) and removed the state file. **One real bug caught only by this live run, not by the unit tests** (consistent with this session's established pattern that networked/process-orchestration code needs live verification, not just unit tests): a leftover reference to the renamed `datetime` import crashed `main()` at the final state-write step, after both servers had already started successfully — meaning a crash there would have left orphaned, untracked server processes with no way to `--stop` them cleanly. Fixed and re-verified end-to-end before considering this done. |
| W45 | completed | **Fix `/api/fixtures` to source real historical results, not just live-scheduled fixtures (found 2026-07-19 by actually using `launch_sandbox.py` (W44) against `SANDBOX_DATE=2025-03-08`).** `GET /api/fixtures` (`app/backend/main.py:125-134`) unconditionally calls `FootballDataClient.get_fixtures()` (status=`SCHEDULED`), never `.get_results()` (status=`FINISHED`) — confirmed both methods already exist and both work against the live football-data.org API (`get_results()` is exactly what `scripts/sandbox_runbook.py`, W31, already calls directly for a historical date). Because of this, `/api/fixtures` structurally returns nothing for any date range that's entirely in the past, regardless of whether real match data exists for it — this is not a data-availability problem, it's a status-filter problem. Three symptoms of the identical root cause, all reproduced live in the same session: (1) **Dashboard** shows blank for `SANDBOX_DATE=2025-03-08` even though 6 real E0 fixtures played that day (`DashboardPage`'s `getFixtures(today, today)`, `MatchUI.tsx:583`); (2) **Match Explorer** search for "Liverpool" returns nothing despite Liverpool having played real fixtures within the 90-day sandbox window, because the entire window (`asOf` to `asOf`+90) is in the past relative to real wall-clock time (`MatchExplorerPage`, `MatchUI.tsx:660`); (3) **the "log a bet manually" search bar** searching "Chelsea" returns nothing for the identical reason — `ManualBetForm` runs the exact same `getFixtures(from, from+90)` pattern (`BetTracker.tsx:40-41`). This was flagged as a known, accepted limitation in `app_techspec.md` §3/§9.1 when it only affected the Dashboard's rarely-used past-date edge case; now that W44 makes it easy to actually browse a sandbox date interactively, it turns out to break three separate surfaces, not one, and warrants a real fix rather than continuing to document around it. **Acceptance:** `GET /api/fixtures` determines, per requested date, whether it falls before, on, or after real wall-clock "today" (not the sandbox override — football-data.org's own SCHEDULED/FINISHED status reflects real match completion, independent of what the app is pretending "today" is) and calls `get_results()` for the past portion of the range and `get_fixtures()` for the future portion, merging both when a range spans the boundary; a request for `SANDBOX_DATE=2025-03-08` on the Dashboard shows that day's 6 real fixtures with real scores; a Match Explorer/manual-bet-log search for "Liverpool"/"Chelsea" within a wholly-past sandbox window returns their real historical fixtures; the existing real-world (non-sandbox) forward-looking behavior is unchanged when the requested range is entirely in the future. | Size S · Milestone M11 · Depends on: W05 (completed). **Completion notes (2026-07-19):** `app/backend/main.py` gained `_current_real_date()` (genuine `datetime.now(timezone.utc).date()`, deliberately not `sandbox_clock.sandbox_now()`, since football-data.org's own SCHEDULED/FINISHED status reflects real-world match completion regardless of what the app is pretending "today" is) and `_split_fixture_date_range()`, a pure helper splitting `[date_from, date_to]` into a results-sub-range and a fixtures-sub-range relative to real "today." TDD: 3 new tests in `test_fixtures_endpoint.py` (wholly-past, wholly-future, boundary-spanning) confirmed to fail against pre-fix code (`AttributeError` on the not-yet-existing helper) before passing post-fix. Full suite: 514 passed/23 skipped/1 deselected, zero regressions. **Code-quality review caught a real correctness gap in the first version**: the initial split treated "today" as future-only (results range stopped at yesterday), so a same-day match that had already finished (e.g. an early kickoff, viewed later) would silently vanish from the Dashboard's actual hot path (`getFixtures(today, today)`) — directly undercutting this fix's own goal. Fixed in a follow-up commit: a range including today now queries today from *both* `get_results()` and `get_fixtures()`, since there's no way to know a same-day match's status without asking both; added 10 direct unit tests for `_split_fixture_date_range()` (inverted range, malformed date, single-sided omission, all boundary-exact cases) per the review's second finding, previously only covered indirectly through HTTP round-trip tests. Final suite: 525 passed, zero regressions. Commits `e34c0ef`, `1700320`. |
| W46 | completed | **Empty-window fallback: show the next N upcoming/nearest matches instead of a blank Dashboard (found 2026-07-19, same session as W45).** Today, if `/api/fixtures` returns an empty list for the requested window — real off-season with no scheduled fixtures, or (until W45 lands) a past sandbox date — the Dashboard renders nothing but "No E0 fixtures today," with no path to see anything else without manually navigating to Match Explorer and guessing a date. **Acceptance:** when the Dashboard's same-day fixture query returns empty, it falls back to fetching and displaying up to 10 matches from the nearest non-empty window going forward from the requested date (real mode: the next real upcoming fixtures, however many days out that turns out to be; sandbox mode once W45 lands: the next real historical matchday(s) after the sandbox date, sourced the same way W45 fixes Match Explorer's window) — clearly labeled as "next matches" rather than presented as if they were today's, so the distinction stays honest; the existing non-empty case (today has real fixtures) is unchanged. | Size S · Milestone M11 · Depends on: W45. **Completion notes (2026-07-19):** `DashboardPage` (`MatchUI.tsx`) gained a `nextMatches: Match[] | null` tri-state (not-yet-checked / checked-empty / checked-found). When the same-day query returns empty, a second `getFixtures(today, today+90days)` call fires (reusing `MatchExplorerPage`'s existing 90-day window precedent), sorted client-side by kickoff (API order isn't guaranteed) and capped at 10, rendered under an explicit "No E0 fixtures today — next matches:" label — kept visually distinct from today's matches per the acceptance's honesty requirement. The new fallback fetch reuses W42's `cancelled`-flag guard a second, independent time (re-checked after its own `await`, not just the primary fetch's), since it's itself a second async call within the same effect run that could race a superseded `asOf`/`retryTick` change. TDD: 4 new tests in `MatchUI.emptyFallback.test.tsx`, including a race test proving a stale fallback response from a cancelled run can't overwrite a fresher one — 3/4 confirmed to fail against pre-fix code (the 4th, "no wasted fetch when today already has matches," legitimately passed pre-fix too, since that specific behavior was never broken — verified this claim directly rather than assuming it). Full suite: 33/33 passing, `tsc --noEmit` clean. **Code-quality review caught a real UX regression**: if the fallback fetch itself failed, the shared `try/catch` replaced the already-correctly-fetched "0 fixtures today" state with a misleading top-level error implying the whole page failed to load, when only the secondary best-effort fallback had. Fixed in a follow-up commit: the fallback fetch now has its own `try/catch` that degrades to the existing "checked, found nothing" empty state rather than surfacing an error — new regression test confirmed this fails against the pre-follow-up code (renders "Could not load today's fixtures.") and passes with the fix. Final suite: 34/34 passing. Commits `2cb56b4`, `2550e69`. |
| W47 | completed | **Frontend never actually reads the recommendation cache — every card expansion and every Match Analysis page load triggers a live agent call, not just the documented "regenerate now" escape hatch (found 2026-07-19, discussing W44/W45's live sandbox session).** Decision D2a (`app_user_stories.md`, "Confirmed So Far") explicitly designed recommendations to be precomputed on a schedule (W09's EOD batch, W10's T-30 refresh) with the dashboard/API reading exclusively from `RecommendationCache` (W11) in the normal path — `GET /api/recommendations/{match_id}` is a cache-only read (`app/backend/main.py`, confirmed: 404 on a miss, never calls `run_agent()`), and `POST /api/recommendations` (`generateRecommendation` in `lib/api.ts`) is meant to be the manual, occasional "regenerate now" fallback. The frontend already has a correctly-implemented, ready-to-use `getCachedRecommendation(matchId, date)` wrapper (`lib/api.ts:59-70`, returns `null` on 404, throws on any other failure) — but it is **imported nowhere and called nowhere** in `MatchUI.tsx`. Both real call sites unconditionally call `generateRecommendation` (the live-agent path) instead: `MatchCard`'s `handleExpand()` (`MatchUI.tsx:443-463`) gates only on the in-memory client-side `match.hasRecommendation` flag (which prevents a *second* call within the same page session, but never checks whether the server already has a cached result) before calling `generateRecommendation`; `MatchAnalysisPage`'s `load()` (`MatchUI.tsx:855-859`) calls `generateRecommendation` unconditionally on every page load/navigation, with no cache check at all. Net effect: every single time a user views a match — including a match the EOD batch or a prior view already generated a recommendation for — the app makes a fresh ~20-30s live Ollama+Tavily call and writes a new cache row, rather than instantly reading the existing one. Separately, and not itself a bug: `ENABLE_SCHEDULER` defaults off (`app/backend/main.py:71`, a deliberate choice — logged, not silent — to stop every `TestClient(app)`/backend boot from non-deterministically firing a real live EOD batch), so even after this is fixed, the cache stays empty unless an operator explicitly opts in; this story is scoped to the frontend wiring gap only, not to changing that default. **Acceptance:** both `MatchCard`'s expand handler and `MatchAnalysisPage`'s `load()` call `getCachedRecommendation(match_id, date)` first; on a hit, render it immediately with no live call; only on a `null` (cache miss) does it fall back to `generateRecommendation`, matching W04's own original stated intent ("triggers the real 'regenerate now' call *if nothing's cached yet*") which the current code never actually implements; a test mocks a cache hit and asserts `generateRecommendation` is never called; a test mocks a cache miss and asserts the existing generate-and-render behavior is preserved unchanged. | Size S · Milestone M11 · Depends on: W11 (completed). **Completion notes (2026-07-19):** Both `MatchCard.handleExpand()` and `MatchAnalysisPage.load()` now call `getCachedRecommendation(matchId, date)` first, applying a hit via the existing `applyRecommendation` helper (which already sets `hasRecommendation: true` regardless of source, so a second expand doesn't re-check the cache unnecessarily) and falling back to the unchanged `generateRecommendation` call only on `null`. A thrown cache-check error (distinct from a 404-derived `null`) is caught locally and also treated as a miss — reasoned as the more robust choice since the cache check is a speed optimization, not the source of truth, and `generateRecommendation` is a fully valid, already-tested fallback either way; this mirrors W46's own "degrade quietly" choice for its comparable fallback-fetch failure. TDD: 4 new tests (2 per call site — cache-hit skips the live call, cache-miss falls back and renders unchanged) confirmed to fail against pre-fix code (which never imports `getCachedRecommendation` at all) before passing post-fix. Spec-compliance review specifically scrutinized whether adding a second sequential `await` (the cache check) ahead of the pre-existing, already-unguarded `generateRecommendation` call meaningfully widens the race window neither call site has ever had a `cancelled`-flag guard for (unlike `DashboardPage`/`MatchExplorerPage`, W42) — concluded the net effect is neutral-to-positive: the new common case (cache hit) *shrinks* the window versus the full ~20-30s live call, and the cache-miss path only adds a fast DB-backed round-trip ahead of an unchanged 20-30s window. The underlying missing-guard gap is real pre-existing tech debt, out of scope for this story, not introduced or worsened by it. Code-quality review approved with non-blocking notes (some duplication between the two call sites' identical cache-first logic, and genuine backend failures being indistinguishable from cache misses in the UI) — both explicitly assessed as consistent with, not worse than, existing accepted patterns in this file (`updateMatch`-style duplication, W46's same silent-fallback style), left as-is. Full suite: 38/38 passing, `tsc --noEmit` clean. Commit `0db66de`. |
| W48 | completed | **Sandbox mode shows real match results/settled status for fixtures that are still "in the future" relative to the sandbox's own pretend `asOf`, instead of treating them as upcoming and bettable (found 2026-07-20, testing `SANDBOX_DATE=2026-03-08` against real wall-clock today 2026-07-20).** `fixtureToMatch()` (`app/frontend/components/MatchUI.tsx:92-100`) derives `Match.status` purely from the real-world `fixture.status === "FINISHED"` check, with no awareness of `asOf`/`sandboxMode` at all — confirmed by reading the function signature, which doesn't even take an `asOf` parameter. `MatchCard`'s `isCompleted` (`MatchUI.tsx:446`) then drives the visible "Result"/"Settled" display (`MatchUI.tsx:500,520,527`) straight from that real-world status. Since `SANDBOX_DATE=2026-03-08` is genuinely in the past relative to real wall-clock time (2026-07-20, confirmed via `date -u`), every real E0 fixture between 2026-03-08 and today has already actually been played and carries a real `FINISHED` status with a real score in `raw_matches` (confirmed: 5 real finished fixtures on 2026-03-14 alone) — including fixtures dated *after* the sandbox's own pretend "today," which the sandbox environment is supposed to treat as not-yet-happened. Two concrete, reported symptoms of this one root cause: (1) **the Dashboard shows a final score ("Result") for matches after sandbox-today** — this is exactly the kind of future-outcome leakage the sandbox environment (W27-31) and the agent's own two leakage defenses (`agent_techspec.md` §9.1/§10, §18.7) exist to prevent, just leaking through a different surface (the raw fixture list, not an agent web-search result) that neither of those defenses covers. (2) **the bet recommendation UI looks unavailable for these matches** — `isCompleted` drives the pre-expand label from "Not yet generated" to "Settled" (`MatchUI.tsx:500`), which reads as "nothing to generate here," even though `handleExpand()` (`MatchUI.tsx:449-463`) doesn't actually gate on `isCompleted` and would still attempt cache-check-then-generate if clicked — **not independently confirmed whether generation genuinely fails or just looks unavailable**, since reproducing this live risked interfering with the user's own running session; this needs to be verified explicitly as part of the fix, not assumed to be purely cosmetic. Since `MatchCard` is shared across `DashboardPage`'s primary list, `DashboardPage`'s W46 fallback list, and `MatchExplorerPage`'s search results (`MatchUI.tsx:700,710,802`), this affects all three surfaces identically whenever any of their result sets includes a fixture dated after `asOf` that has already really been played — which W46's fallback (by construction, since it searches forward from `asOf`) and Match Explorer (whenever its whole 90-day window lands in the past relative to real wall-clock time, as W45's completion notes already describe) will commonly do. **Explicitly not in scope for this bug:** real match-completion status remains correct and necessary for auto-settlement (W13, `settlement.py`) and for the primary same-day query when the sandbox date itself has already had matches kick off earlier that same day — this is purely about how `status`/`result` should be *displayed and interacted with* relative to the sandbox's own pretend timeline, not about changing what "real" data the backend sources. **Acceptance:** in sandbox mode, a fixture whose `kickoffIso` date is strictly after `asOf`'s date renders as `status: "upcoming"` (no score shown, "Odds"/"Not yet generated" labeling, same as a genuinely future real fixture) regardless of its real-world `FINISHED` status; the same fixture's recommendation can still be generated/cached exactly as any other upcoming match, confirmed by an explicit test (not just inferred from `handleExpand()`'s existing logic); outside sandbox mode, or for a fixture on/before `asOf`, behavior is unchanged; a regression test using a real-shaped `FINISHED` fixture dated after a sandbox `asOf` confirms it renders as upcoming, not completed. | Size S · Milestone M11 · Depends on: W45, W46 (both completed). **Completion notes (2026-07-20):** `fixtureToMatch()` (`MatchUI.tsx`) gained optional `asOf`/`sandboxMode` parameters; a fixture strictly after `asOf`'s date (in sandbox mode) now computes `status: "upcoming"` with `result: undefined`, regardless of its real-world `FINISHED` status — gated via a new `isFutureInSandbox` check. The date-comparison logic reuses `formatDay()`'s pre-existing UTC-vs-local getter branching (extracted into a shared `dayDiff()` helper, returning the raw signed day count instead of a formatted label) rather than hand-writing a second, separately-fallible version — deliberately avoiding re-deriving W30's getter-mismatch bug class in a new spot. All three `fixtureToMatch()` call sites (`DashboardPage` primary fetch, `DashboardPage`'s W46 fallback fetch, `MatchExplorerPage`'s search fetch) now pass `asOf`/`sandboxMode` through; `MatchCard` itself needed no changes, since its existing `isCompleted = match.status === "completed"` and `result` rendering automatically inherit the corrected value. TDD: 4 new tests in `MatchUI.dateboundary.test.tsx` — future-FINISHED renders upcoming with no score; sandbox-mode-off still shows the real result (regression guard); on-(not-after)-`asOf` still shows the real result (regression guard for the explicit "not in scope" carve-out); and a recommendation genuinely still generates/caches for a future-in-sandbox fixture, exercising the real `handleExpand()` → `getCachedRecommendation` → `generateRecommendation`-fallback flow end-to-end rather than assuming it from the lack of an `isCompleted` gate — this directly resolves the story's "not independently confirmed" caveat about symptom (2). Spec-compliance review individually verified all 4 tests against pre-fix code: 2 genuinely fail (the future-renders-upcoming case and the recommendation-still-generates case), 2 legitimately pass even pre-fix since they're regression guards for behavior that was never broken (sandbox-off and on-`asOf` cases). Full suite: 42/42 passing, `tsc --noEmit` clean. | W49 | completed | **On-demand recommendation generation (`POST /api/recommendations`, the manual "regenerate now" path `MatchCard`/`MatchAnalysisPage` actually use) never fetches or attaches available odds, unlike the scheduled EOD/T-30 pipeline — producing avoidable `insufficient_data` results even when real odds exist (found 2026-07-20, live-reproduced clicking a sandbox "next match" card).** Diagnosed with a real click-through, network capture, and a direct DB check, not inferred: clicked a real fixture (Sunderland vs Brighton Hove, 2026-03-14) rendered as upcoming per W48's fix; the request genuinely completed in ~25s (not stuck) and correctly transitioned the card from "Not yet generated" to a real `Insufficient Data` result — but the captured `POST /api/recommendations` body was `{"home_team":"Sunderland","away_team":"Brighton Hove","date":"2026-03-14","league":"E0","match_id":"538075"}`, **no `odds` field at all**, and the response's `limitations` read `["Unable to find bookmaker odds", "Forecast tools returned errors"]` — yet `raw_matches` has real odds for this exact fixture (`odds_h=3.3, odds_d=3.3, odds_a=2.25`, confirmed via direct query). Root cause: `create_recommendation()` (`app/backend/main.py:206-226`) calls `recommendations.run_agent(request.to_match_info())` directly — `RecommendationRequest.odds` (`recommendations.py:114-132`) is purely caller-supplied with no server-side fallback, and neither `MatchCard.handleExpand()` nor `MatchAnalysisPage.load()` (`MatchUI.tsx`) ever populate it. Compare `eod_batch.py`'s `run_eod_batch()` (`app/backend/eod_batch.py:82-96`), which explicitly calls `build_odds_client().get_odds()` (`scheduler_wiring.py:53-60` — resolves to W28's `HistoricalOddsClient` in sandbox mode, the real `OddsAPIClient` in live mode) and `match_odds()` (canonical team-name matching, the BUG-015 fix) *before* calling `run_agent()`, attaching `match_info["odds"]` when a match is found. The manual path has none of this — the agent is left to find odds itself via `web_search`, which cannot succeed for a historical/sandboxed match (no bookmaker publishes odds for a match that's already been played) and frequently fails for real matches too depending on what the LLM's search turns up. This is not sandbox-exclusive: any live "regenerate now" click for a real upcoming fixture also skips a `build_odds_client()` fetch today, relying entirely on the agent's own web search rather than the app's own odds pipeline. **Acceptance:** `create_recommendation()` fetches odds via `build_odds_client()`/`match_odds()` (reusing `eod_batch.py`'s exact logic, not a reimplementation) and attaches them to `match_info["odds"]` whenever the request didn't already supply `odds` explicitly (preserving the existing override behavior for any caller that does pass odds); a test confirms a sandboxed match with real historical odds in `raw_matches` no longer needs a live web search to find them and produces a real (non-`insufficient_data`-for-this-reason) recommendation; a test confirms explicitly-supplied `odds` in the request still take precedence over the fetched value; a test confirms a match with no odds available anywhere still degrades to the existing no-odds behavior rather than erroring. | Size S · Milestone M11 · Depends on: W07, W09, W28 (all completed). **Completion notes (2026-07-20):** Added `_fetch_odds_for_manual_request()` (`app/backend/main.py`) — genuinely reuses `eod_batch.py`'s `build_odds_client()`/`odds_lookup()`/`match_odds()` (BUG-015's canonical team-name matching included), constructing a throwaway `NormalizedMatch` to satisfy `match_odds()`'s signature since it only reads `home_team`/`away_team` off it (confirmed by reading the function, not assumed). Runs via `run_in_threadpool` (odds clients make real synchronous HTTP/DB calls) only when `request.odds is None`; every failure mode (no client configured, no matching event, client raising) degrades to the pre-existing no-odds behavior rather than a 500. TDD: 5 new tests in `test_recommendations_endpoint.py`, the positive-fetch test confirmed to fail pre-fix (`KeyError: 'odds'`); the 4 degrade-gracefully tests legitimately pass even pre-fix since there was no fetch to fail before this existed — expected, not a red flag. Full suite: 530 passed, no regressions. **Spec-compliance review caught a real bug**: the cache write recorded `request.odds` (the original, still-`None` caller value) instead of `match_info.get("odds")` (what was actually fetched and used) — meaning a fetched-odds generation was cached with `odds={}`, which would spuriously trip `t30_refresh.py`'s "odds unchanged, skip regeneration" dedup check on every later comparison. Fixed in a follow-up commit with its own test, verified to fail against the pre-follow-up code and pass with the fix. **Code-quality review** flagged a real operational concern (manual regeneration now silently draws from the same `CreditCounter`-guarded monthly Odds API budget the scheduler uses — previously zero cost, still safety-margin-protected via `would_exceed()`, but undocumented) plus two style nitpicks (a one-off logging format, `.pop()` vs the suite's `.clear()` convention) — all three addressed in a second follow-up commit. Final suite: 531 passed, zero regressions. Commits `b7d8833`, `c52853d`, `a5988f9`. |
| W50 | completed | **Pre-compute recommendations for a sandbox scenario at launch time, mirroring the live EOD batch, instead of requiring one real ~10-30s agent call per card click (requested 2026-07-20, "I hope to have them pre-computed, just like in the live case").** Today, `launch_sandbox.py` (W44) boots the app but leaves the recommendation cache empty — `ENABLE_SCHEDULER` defaults off deliberately (`app/backend/main.py:71`, to stop every backend boot/test run from non-deterministically firing a real live EOD batch), so nothing pre-populates recommendations for the sandbox date's fixtures the way a real user's EOD batch (W09) would for tomorrow's real fixtures. | W51 | completed | **`--precompute` (W50) only covers the exact `SANDBOX_DATE`, not the fallback window the Dashboard actually falls back to when that date is empty — leaving most of what a user actually sees still "Not yet generated" (found 2026-07-20, live-reproduced against the user's own running session).** `fetch_sandbox_fixtures()` (`scripts/launch_sandbox.py`) queries `get_results(date_from=date_str, date_to=date_str)` — a single day. The two cards showing real data in the reported screenshot were leftover cache entries from earlier diagnostic testing (one of them literally the exact fixture used to originally diagnose W49), not from any successful precompute run — confirmed by checking `record_generation`'s cached `limitations` for that entry (`"No bookmaker odds found"`, the pre-W49 failure mode) and, separately, by triggering one fresh generation for a currently-blank card through the live session's own `POST /api/recommendations`, which came back a clean `direct_bet` with real odds and no odds-related limitations — proving W49's fix works correctly on fresh generations and the "lot of insufficient_data" the user was seeing is dominated by stale pre-W49 cache, not a currently-live defect. **Acceptance:** `precompute_recommendations()` mirrors `DashboardPage`'s exact fallback logic (`MatchUI.tsx`, W46) — query the exact sandbox date first; if empty, fall back to the same 90-day-forward window, sorted nearest-first, capped at the same 10 matches the Dashboard actually renders — so precompute's scope matches what a user opening the browser will actually see, not a strictly-smaller or differently-scoped set. | W52 | completed | **`/api/fixtures` has no error handling around the football-data.org call and no response caching, so the shared rate-limited client is independently re-hit by every page on every mount — ordinary navigation exhausts the ~10 req/min free-tier budget and the next fetch 500s, with the Bets page search silently swallowing this into an empty result (found 2026-07-21, live-reproduced: "Match searching in Matches and Bets page does not show any matches" / "After clicking into Matches and switching back to Dashboard, no fixture is shown").** Confirmed via the user's own running sandbox backend log (`/tmp/fpai_sandbox_backend.log`): repeated `requests.exceptions.HTTPError: 429 Client Error` from `football-data.org`, each one propagating uncaught through `FootballDataClient._get_matches()`'s `response.raise_for_status()` (`app/backend/football_data_client.py:115`) and `/api/fixtures` (`app/backend/main.py:177-202`, no try/except around either `client.get_results`/`client.get_fixtures` call) into an unhandled-exception 500. `_fixtures_client` (`main.py:41`) is a single module-level singleton shared across every request; `DashboardPage` (today + a possible 90-day fallback), `MatchExplorerPage` (its own 90-day window), and `ManualBetForm`/`BetTracker.tsx:41` (another 90-day window) each independently call `getFixtures()` fresh on every mount with zero caching or de-duplication, so normal navigation across Dashboard → Matches → Bets within the same rate window collectively exhausts the shared budget. `_RateLimiter` (`football_data_client.py:43-72`) is reactive, not preventive, for the first several calls of a burst — `_remaining` starts `None` and only throttles once a response header has actually reported exhaustion, so it cannot stop a fast burst of navigations from tripping the limit before the first 429 comes back. Downstream, `getFixtures()` (`lib/api.ts:19-30`) correctly throws an `ApiError` on the 500, and `MatchExplorerPage` shows a visible error banner — but `ManualBetForm`'s fetch (`BetTracker.tsx:41-47`) does `.catch(() => setFixtures([]))`, silently discarding the failure with no error surfaced at all, so its search bar just shows nothing. **Acceptance:** `/api/fixtures` no longer 500s on an upstream rate-limit — repeated identical-range requests within a short window are served from a short-lived in-process cache instead of re-hitting football-data.org, and any `HTTPError` that does reach the endpoint (cache miss during an active rate-limit window) degrades to a clear, non-500 response rather than an unhandled exception; `ManualBetForm` surfaces a visible error state on a genuine fetch failure instead of silently rendering an empty search. A test confirms two `/api/fixtures` calls for the same date range within the cache TTL only hit the underlying client once; a test confirms an `HTTPError` from the client no longer produces an unhandled 500; a test confirms `ManualBetForm` shows an error message (not just an empty result list) when its fixture fetch fails. | Size S · Milestone M11 · Depends on: W45 (completed). **Completion notes (2026-07-21):** Added a 60-second in-process TTL cache in `main.py` (`_cached_fixture_call()`), keyed per results/fixtures sub-call rather than per merged `/api/fixtures` response, so it composes correctly with `_split_fixture_date_range()`'s today-spanning split; a caught `requests.exceptions.HTTPError` now raises `HTTPException(503, ...)` instead of reaching FastAPI's default handler as a raw 500, and a failed call is never written to the cache (so a single 429 can't get "frozen" and returned as if genuinely confirmed for the next 60s). `ManualBetForm` (`BetTracker.tsx`) gained a `fixturesError` state rendered via the same `ErrorState` component `DashboardPage`/`MatchExplorerPage` already use. TDD: 2 new backend tests (dedup call-count assertion, 503-not-500 on a raised `HTTPError`) confirmed to fail pre-fix; 1 new frontend test (`BetTracker.fixtureError.test.tsx`) confirmed to fail pre-fix (`ManualBetForm` had no error UI at all). Full suite: 196 backend / 43 frontend passing at first cut. **Code-quality review caught two real gaps**, both fixed in a follow-up commit with their own tests: (1) no in-flight de-duplication — React StrictMode's dev-mode double-effect-invocation could still fire two real upstream calls for one logical request before either resolved, since the cache-miss-then-fetch decision had no lock between the check and the fetch; fixed by registering the in-flight `asyncio.Task` in the cache before awaiting it, with a genuine concurrency test (two real OS threads against `TestClient`, since this codebase has no `pytest-asyncio` setup) proving a second concurrent request awaits the same task rather than issuing its own; (2) TTL expiry itself was untested (only within-TTL dedup was) — fixed with a patchable `_fixture_cache_now()` (mirroring this file's existing `_current_real_date()` convention) and a test advancing a fake clock past 60s to confirm re-fetch. Also added a Retry button to `ManualBetForm`'s new error state (reusing `DashboardPage`/`MatchExplorerPage`'s existing `retryTick` pattern), which the first cut had omitted despite the new 503 message explicitly promising a retry the UI couldn't otherwise deliver. Final suite: 198 backend / 44 frontend passing, zero regressions. Commits `21e6bf9`, `954fe82`. |
| W53 | completed | **The initial fixture list never reflects precomputed/cached recommendation status — every card shows "Not yet generated" until individually clicked, even immediately after a full `--precompute` run (found 2026-07-21, reported: "before I click on the cards, the status still shows Not generated, even with --precompute").** `fixtureToMatch()` (`app/frontend/components/MatchUI.tsx:92-136`) unconditionally sets `hasRecommendation: false` (line 124) when building each `Match` from a raw `Fixture` — it has no cache awareness at all. W47 wired `getCachedRecommendation()` into exactly two call sites, `MatchCard.handleExpand()` and `MatchAnalysisPage.load()`, and both are lazy: they only run when a user expands/opens a specific card. | W54 | completed | **Sandbox fallback-window precompute uses the sandbox as-of date for later fixtures, so fresh precomputed recommendations are stored under the wrong cache key and generated without the right historical odds (found 2026-07-22 while investigating `python scripts/launch_sandbox.py 2026-03-08 --precompute`).** W51 correctly made `fetch_sandbox_fixtures()` fall back from an empty exact date (`2026-03-08`) to the next 10 fixtures in the 90-day window, but `run_eod_batch()` still treats its single `date_str` argument as both the batch date and the fixture date. That was safe for the live EOD path, where every fixture is for tomorrow, but wrong for sandbox fallback: the newest precompute rows in `app/data/sandbox/recommendation_cache.db` were written as `date=2026-03-08` for match IDs the Dashboard actually queries by their real dates (`2026-03-14`, `2026-03-15`, `2026-03-16`), so W53's initial-list cache lookup misses the fresh rows and shows older stale generations or 404s. The same wrong date is passed into `match_info["date"]`, making the agent forecast and snapshot key use the sandbox date instead of kickoff date, and `HistoricalOddsClient(sandbox_date=2026-03-08)` returns no odds for the March 14–16 fallback fixtures, so every fresh row has `odds_json={}` and many recommendations degrade to no/insufficient data. **Acceptance:** sandbox fallback precompute records each fixture under the fixture's own date, passes that same fixture date into `run_agent()`, and fetches historical odds for each fixture date (or otherwise supplies per-fixture odds directly) while preserving the live EOD behavior for true one-day batches. Regression tests cover: fallback fixtures with dates later than `SANDBOX_DATE` are cached under their actual dates; `match_info["date"]` equals the fixture date; historical odds are matched for those fixture dates instead of only the sandbox as-of date; W53's initial-list cache check can see the freshly precomputed rows with no stale-cache dependency. | Size M · Milestone M11 · Depends on: W51, W53. **Completion notes (2026-07-26):** Reproduced live first — `precompute_recommendations()` had just written `538075`/`538076`/`538078`/`538080`-`538084` (E0) plus several SWE fixtures all under `date='2026-03-08'`, while `raw_matches`/the fixtures API confirm their real kickoffs are `2026-03-14`/`15`/`16` — a 100% cache-key miss for every fallback fixture, every time. Root cause exactly as scoped: `run_eod_batch()` (`app/backend/eod_batch.py`) used the single `date_str` argument for `match_info["date"]`, `cache.record_generation(date=...)`, *and* the one upfront `odds_client.get_odds()` call, all three implicitly assuming every fixture in the batch shares one date — true for the live scheduler (always "tomorrow") and an exact-date sandbox run, false for the W51 fallback window. Fix: new `_fixture_date(fixture)` helper (`fixture.utc_date[:10]`) is now used for both `match_info["date"]` and the cache write, per fixture. Odds fetching branches on whether every fixture's own date already equals `date_str` (`fixture_dates <= {date_str}`) — if so, the *original* single `get_odds(sport_key=...)` call is preserved byte-for-byte (verified via the pre-existing `test_get_odds_is_called_with_an_explicit_epl_sport_key` continuing to pass unmodified, asserting exactly one call with only `sport_key`); otherwise, odds are fetched once per distinct fixture date via a new optional `date` parameter threaded onto both `HistoricalOddsClient.get_odds()` (actually overrides `self._sandbox_date` for that call) and `OddsAPIClient.get_odds()` (accepted, ignored — interface parity only, since live current-odds has no historical-date concept, mirroring the existing `sport_key`-on-`HistoricalOddsClient` pattern). TDD: 3 new `test_eod_batch.py` tests (fallback fixture cached under its own date not `date_str`; `match_info["date"]` equals the fixture date; odds fetched per distinct date when fixtures span several, proven via two fixtures each getting their own, correctly-matched odds) + 2 new `test_historical_odds_client.py` tests (`date` param overrides the constructor default; omitting it preserves prior behavior), all watched failing for the right reason (`TypeError` on the not-yet-existing `date` kwarg; assertions matching the old wrong-date behavior) before the fix. All 18 pre-existing `eod_batch`/`historical_odds_client` tests still pass completely unmodified — in particular every exact-call-shape assertion (`assert_called_once_with(sport_key=...)`, no `date`) — proving the single-date fast path is untouched. Full suite: 811 passed / 1 skipped, zero regressions. **Verified live against real data end-to-end** (real football-data.org fallback fixtures — confirmed `used_fallback=True`, 10 real E0 fixtures found for the March 14-16 window — real `HistoricalOddsClient`, real `llama3.1:8b`): ran `run_eod_batch` against one real fallback fixture (Sunderland v Brighton Hove, kickoff `2026-03-14`) with `date_str="2026-03-08"` — the resulting cache row landed under `date='2026-03-14'` (confirmed via direct DuckDB query, `generated_at` matching the run), not `2026-03-08`. Pre-fix stale rows under `date='2026-03-08'` for these same match IDs were left in place (harmless orphans — nothing queries that key for these matches going forward) rather than purged, since cleaning up historical bad data wasn't part of this story's scope. |

W55's live research found the real per-endpoint costs differ: `/events` (fixture discovery) is **0 credits/call** (confirmed via the `x-requests-used` response header), `/odds` is **1 credit/call** (matches EPL's existing 1×1 formula), but `/scores` (settlement results, needed only for SWE since EPL's results still come free from football-data.org) is **2 credits/call** — a genuinely new, non-trivial cost category EPL never had. **Acceptance:** an updated hand-computed monthly estimate covers all three SWE call types at their real confirmed costs (1 EOD odds call/day + 1 T-30 odds call per SWE fixture, both 1 credit each; settlement `/scores` polling at 2 credits/call, however often that ends up running) alongside EPL's unchanged existing usage, written into W07's story or a dedicated note; `CreditCounter`'s existing safety-margin skip-gracefully behavior is confirmed sufficient at the new volume, or a follow-up story is flagged if the 2-credit `/scores` cost makes settlement polling frequency the actual binding constraint rather than the EOD/T-30 odds calls. | Size XS · Milestone M12 · Depends on: W58. **Completion notes (2026-07-23):** Hand-computed monthly estimate, EPL unchanged + Sweden added: **EPL** (~10 fixtures/week, unchanged) — EOD odds 1 call/day × 30 = 30 credits/month + T-30 odds ~43 matches/month × 1 = 43 credits/month → **≈73 credits/month**. **Sweden** — real pilot data (A36's 24-match corpus, 2026-07-01 to 2026-07-20) shows ≈1.2 matches/day ≈ 36/month: EOD SWE odds 1 call/day × 30 = 30 credits/month; T-30 SWE odds ≈36 × 1 = 36 credits/month; settlement `/scores` is on-demand (no schedule, W13), so its true cost depends on click/poll frequency — a deliberately pessimistic upper bound (settlement invoked once for every distinct SWE match-day) is ≈30 calls/month × 2 credits = 60 credits/month → Sweden **≈126 credits/month worst-case**. **Combined worst-case ≈199 credits/month against the 500 limit** — comfortably within budget (≈301 credits/month of headroom remaining, well clear of `CreditCounter`'s existing 50-credit safety margin) but a real, permanent reduction in slack from the EPL-only ≈427-credit headroom noted in W07/the Operational section above. **No code change needed** — `CreditCounter`'s existing `would_exceed()`/safety-margin skip-gracefully behavior already covers this new volume with room to spare; no follow-up story required. Settlement's on-demand (not scheduled) nature means the 60-credit worst-case for `/scores` is the actual lever to watch if usage patterns ever change (e.g. if settlement were later put on its own schedule) — flagged here for future reference rather than acted on now, since it's still well within budget as-is. |

| W70 | completed | **Bridge the webapp's sandbox agent-run mode-selection to the `agent-snapshot` corpus, and fix sandbox recordings not surviving a backend restart.** Investigated 2026-07-24 while checking whether the webapp would work with a new Anthropic-API-based `agent-snapshot` corpus (Aug 2025–May 2026, A34's rebaseline job): it doesn't, today — two structurally separate systems exist. `app/backend/recommendations.py`'s sandbox mode reads/writes only `data/agent_snapshots/sandbox/<home>__<away>__<date>/`, never the corpus's `data/agent_snapshots/E0/<match_id>/` or `data/agent_snapshots/SWE/<match_id>/`. Separately, even the sandbox's own recordings aren't reused across a restart: `_sandbox_recorded_matches` is an in-memory `set()` that starts empty every process start, so record-vs-replay is decided by "have I, this process, already recorded this match" rather than "does a recording already exist on disk" — a fresh process re-records (and silently overwrites) rather than replaying. **Acceptance:** mode selection checks disk in priority order — (1) a complete recording already in the sandbox partition → replay; (2) if not, the fixture's real `raw_matches.match_id` (resolved via the existing `TeamNameMapper`, same tool W06 uses for the identical team-name-matching problem) has a complete recording in the `E0`/`SWE` corpus → replay directly from there; (3) otherwise, record fresh into the sandbox partition, unchanged from today. A team-name-mapping miss degrades to "no corpus match, fall through to record" — never an error, matching this codebase's existing unmapped-team-warns-not-crashes precedent. The existing W43 fallback (a replay-mode `SnapshotMissingError` retries once in record mode) is preserved and now also covers a corpus-replay miss. A test confirms: (a) a sandbox-partition recording from a prior "process" is replayed by a fresh one (the restart-persistence fix, verifiable without touching the corpus at all); (b) a fixture with no sandbox recording but a matching complete corpus entry replays from the corpus, making zero live calls; (c) a fixture with neither falls through to record, unchanged from today's behavior. | Size M · Milestone M14 · Depends on: none (uses existing `TeamNameMapper`/`raw_matches`/`SnapshotStore` — no new infrastructure). **Completion notes (2026-07-25):** `app/backend/recommendations.py` — `_select_sandbox_snapshot_source()` implements the 3-tier priority check, `_lookup_corpus_match_id()` resolves a fixture to `raw_matches.match_id` via `TeamNameMapper`. **Critical bug caught only by driving the real code, not by review of the diff (commit `fc40512`):** the initial implementation (`e965f533`) checked the sandbox partition for a `_complete.json` marker to decide "already recorded," but nothing in the webapp's own record path ever *wrote* that marker — the convention belonged solely to the separate standalone `agent-snapshot` CLI (`main.py`), which writes it into the corpus, never into the sandbox partition. So the restart-persistence fix this story exists to deliver silently didn't work: every sandboxed request, same-process or not, kept re-recording — worse than the in-memory `set()` it replaced (that at least caught same-process repeats). Found by actually driving `_run_agent_in_mode()` end-to-end and checking disk for the marker after a record pass, not by inspection. Fixed by having `_run_agent_in_mode()` write `_complete.json` itself after a successful record-mode pass, mirroring `main.py`'s exact convention; verified with a real (unmocked `SnapshotStore`) round-trip test driving an actual record pass and confirming both the marker write and the resulting replay decision on the next call. A second review round (`c03d715`) found `_lookup_corpus_match_id()` didn't actually honor its own documented "never raises" contract — `DuckDBManager()`/`load_settings()` can raise on a missing/invalid `config.yaml` and `conn.execute()` can raise `duckdb.Error`, neither caught, meaning an uncaught lookup failure would 500 the sandbox request instead of degrading to "no corpus match, record fresh" — fixed, with 2 new tests covering the DB-error paths. Full test count: `test_sandbox_agent_snapshot.py` — 15 passed. Combined backend suite after this story: no regressions. |
| W71 | completed | **Fix SWE historical fixture sourcing so `/api/fixtures` resolves arbitrary past dates, mirroring W45's fix for E0.** Found 2026-07-24 during the same investigation: `SwedenFixturesClient` (Odds-API-backed, W57) can only ever see the last few real days (`GET /scores?daysFrom<=3`, a provider limitation, not a bug in this codebase's own code) — it structurally cannot return fixtures for an arbitrary historical date the way `FootballDataClient.get_results()` already does for E0 (W45). This blocks SWE scenario testing for any of Aug 2025–May 2026 regardless of whether an agent-snapshot recording exists for that date, since the fixture would never even be found. `raw_matches` already has real Allsvenskan history back to 2012 (the ML engine's own ingestion target, `src/ingestion/football_data/sweden_fetcher.py`) — real data was never the gap, only the live-API sourcing path was. **Acceptance:** a new `raw_matches`-backed historical SWE source replaces `SwedenFixturesClient` on the past-date branch of `/api/fixtures` only (the future/fixtures-side branch, which the Odds API *can* serve correctly, is unchanged) — rows converted to the existing `NormalizedMatch` shape (`status="FINISHED"`, goals from the row), tagged `competition="SWE"` (W64's existing tagging convention). Applied unconditionally, like W45, not sandbox-gated — this is a general correctness fix, not a sandbox-only one. A test confirms a real historical SWE date now returns real fixtures via `/api/fixtures` where it previously returned none. | Size S · Milestone M14 · Depends on: none. **Completion notes (2026-07-25):** `app/backend/sweden_fixtures_client.py` gained `historical_results_from_raw_matches()`; `app/backend/main.py`'s past-date branch of `/api/fixtures` now sources SWE from it (commit `f1ef8da`). **Critical cross-stack bug found in code review (commit `e50edeb`), not in the initial implementation or its own tests:** `historical_results_from_raw_matches()` synthesizes midnight-UTC kickoff timestamps (`raw_matches` carries no real kickoff time), and the frontend's `dayDiff()` (`app/frontend/components/MatchUI.tsx`) computed the fixture-side day boundary using the viewer's *local* calendar day instead of branching on `sandboxMode` the way its `asOf`-side computation already did. For a negative-UTC-offset viewer, a midnight-UTC fixture can land on the previous local calendar day, silently flipping W48's leak-prevention guard (`isFutureInSandbox`) and letting a real `FINISHED` result/score render through the sandbox timeline as if it hadn't happened yet — a real, novel leak vector this story's new synthetic-timestamp shape introduced, not caught until review compared the new server-side data shape against the existing frontend guard's actual logic. Fixed by mirroring the `asOf`-side UTC branch on the fixture side of `dayDiff()`, and exporting `dayDiff` for direct testing (6 new tests in `MatchUI.dateboundary.test.tsx` covering the negative-offset case). Same review round also fixed: `historical_results_from_raw_matches()` now wraps its DB call in try/except and degrades to `[]` on failure (mirroring `_lookup_corpus_match_id()`'s never-raises contract from W70) instead of letting a DB failure 500 the whole `/api/fixtures` response and discard already-succeeded E0 results from the same merge; documented the `raw_matches.match_id` vs. `SwedenFixturesClient` Odds-API-event-id id-space discontinuity as a known, accepted limitation, mirroring `settlement.py`'s existing precedent for the analogous E0/SWE case; added a null-`fthg`/`ftag` test case. Test counts: `test_fixtures_endpoint.py` + `test_sweden_fixtures_client.py` — 37 passed combined; frontend `MatchUI.dateboundary.test.tsx` — 11 passed (6 of them `dayDiff`-specific). |

| W73 | completed | **Repeatable scenario-testing runbook across Aug 2025–May 2026, mirroring W31/W44's existing runbook pattern.** Once W70-W72 land, this verifies the whole chain actually works end-to-end across the real requested range, not just in unit tests. **Acceptance:** a new `scripts/scenario_runbook.py` (distinct from `scripts/sandbox_runbook.py`, W31, which documents one specific single-date walkthrough — this one walks a *range*) takes a date range, samples a representative set of dates across it (sized for wall-clock feasibility, same reasoning as W20 — not necessarily every single day), and for each sampled date: launches the sandbox, runs `--precompute` for both leagues, and verifies (a) fixtures were found for at least one league, (b) recommendations were generated via corpus replay specifically — checked by asserting on `SnapshotStore`'s actual mode/outcome, not just "something rendered," so a silent fall-through to a live call wouldn't pass — and (c) the Dashboard/Match Explorer/Match Analysis pages (this repo's current `AppShell`-based redesign) render the results correctly. Produces a written report in this project's existing runbook-documentation style (see W23/W31/W44), covering both leagues across the sampled range, with results recorded honestly (including any dates where the corpus has no recording for that period, rather than treating a gap as a failure of this story's own acceptance criteria — this story verifies the *pipeline* works, not that the corpus has 100% coverage). | Size M · Milestone M14 · Depends on: W70, W71, W72. **Completion notes (2026-07-25):** New `scripts/scenario_runbook.py` — walks a sampled date range, shells out to `launch_sandbox.py <date> --precompute` per date (capturing its stdout+stderr), parses the real, current per-league output lines (`Precompute [<league>]: N real fixture(s) found`/the W51 90-day-fallback line/`nothing to generate.`/the `complete: generated=G skipped=S` tally/`sandbox_agent_replay_miss`), then always stops the sandbox before moving to the next date, including on a launch error or timeout. The parsing logic is a pure function (`parse_precompute_output()`) separated from the subprocess-driving code specifically so it could be unit tested against realistic captured text without a real sandbox launch — `scripts/test_scenario_runbook.py` (10 tests, including one built from real output captured live during this task) covers `sample_dates()` and the parser, including the case the plan's own draft regex would have gotten wrong (the 90-day-fallback line also contains the substring "real fixtures" and would have been double-counted as an exact-date hit against a loose pattern — caught while re-verifying the draft's assumptions against the real script per the plan's own instruction to do so, not guessed). **Step 1 corpus coverage found:** 337 complete E0 recordings, 24 complete SWE recordings under `data/agent_snapshots/` (both keyed by `raw_matches.match_id` hash, not by date) — a real but partial corpus, confirming the plan's expectation that recording was still in progress; the runbook was written to handle this (and a zero-coverage date) without crashing, which it does. **Live run (2026-07-25):** 11 dates sampled every 30 days from 2025-08-01 to 2026-05-31 (`2025-08-01, 2025-08-31, 2025-09-30, 2025-10-30, 2025-11-29, 2025-12-29, 2026-01-28, 2026-02-27, 2026-03-29, 2026-04-28, 2026-05-28`), run in two chunks to fit tool timeout limits (identical `--sample-every-days 30` cadence reproduces the same dates split at 2025-12-29/2026-01-28) — all 11 launches completed with `[OK]` status, zero crashes, zero orphaned sandbox processes. Combined across both leagues: 28 fixtures `generated`, 137 `skipped`, 165 total fixture-generation attempts. Per the known Anthropic-billing constraint noted going into this task, no sampled date produced a full LLM-driven recommendation — **direct raw-output verification on 2 dates (2025-08-31, 2025-09-30, re-run standalone outside the wrapper for full-detail capture, both reproducing the wrapped run's counts exactly)** showed every single `generated` outcome traces to the no-odds `insufficient_data` path (`output_node \| no_forecast \| reason=No odds available`, which structurally never reaches web_search or the LLM), and every `skipped` outcome traces to an external-provider error — the documented Anthropic credit-balance error on the first sampled date checked (2025-08-16, during Step 1/2 calibration), and a Tavily plan-usage-limit error (`This request exceeds your plan's set usage limit`) on later checks, most likely this environment's Tavily quota being consumed by the volume of real calls this task itself made across calibration + the 11-date run + the 2 supplementary direct captures. Both are real, external, already-known-class constraints (not code defects) that the agent's existing exception handling degraded from cleanly in every case observed — no crash, no hang, no corrupted cache entry. **Replay-vs-live-call finding (the part fixture counts alone can't prove):** 3 of the 11 sampled dates (2025-08-31, 2025-11-29, 2026-02-27) logged `sandbox_agent_replay_miss` (4 occurrences total) — direct, positive proof the W70 corpus bridge is functioning: `_select_sandbox_snapshot_source()` found a matching corpus entry via `TeamNameMapper`/`raw_matches` for those specific fixtures and switched to replay mode, only for the granular per-tool-call replay lookup to miss (W43's documented non-reproducible-argument limitation) and correctly fall back to a record-mode retry. No sampled date showed a *clean, complete* corpus replay reaching a full markets-bearing recommendation with zero live calls — an honest limitation of what could be observed today, not a defect: the corpus's real date coverage (Step 1) only partially overlaps the sampled range, and `recommendations.py` has no positive "replay succeeded" log line to key off of even where it might have happened silently. This is accurately reported as current corpus/environment state, not as a failure of the pipeline itself — the corpus-lookup, priority-ordering, and miss-fallback mechanisms W70 built are all directly evidenced as working; only end-to-end "silent full replay" isn't independently provable from today's run given the environment's live-call constraints. Full combined test suite after this story: 850 passed / 1 skipped (backend `tests/` + `app/backend/tests/` + `scripts/`, including this story's own 10 new tests, plus W70's later concurrency-guard fix/regression test); frontend 77 passed, `tsc --noEmit` clean. |

## PHASE 16: Conditional-Market Target Odds Display

> Direct user request (2026-08-06), app-side half of `documents/agent_user_stories.md` Phase 14/A52: once the agent computes what odds would clear a conditional market's value threshold, the app needs to actually show it next to the Conditional/WAIT badge rather than leaving the bettor to guess. Design doc: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-06-conditional-market-target-odds-design.md`. **Numbering note:** numbered 15 here on `main`; `feature/la-liga-integration` numbers the same stories as its own Phase 16 — expect a renumber when that branch merges.

| ID | Status | Description | Comments |
|---|---|---|---|
| W83 | completed | **Thread the agent's new `target_odds` field through the backend response.** `MarketRecommendationOut` (`app/backend/recommendations.py`) gains `target_odds: float \| None`, passed straight through by `validate_and_degrade` alongside the existing per-market fields — same shape as W15's `feature_completeness` plumbing, no new validation logic needed since A52 already guarantees the field's correctness/nullability. **Acceptance:** a cached/live recommendation whose agent-side payload carries a non-null `target_odds` on a conditional market returns it unchanged in the API response; a market without it (any pre-A52 cached row, or a non-conditional market) degrades to `None` rather than raising; regression test covers both. | Size XS · Milestone M15 · Depends on: `documents/agent_user_stories.md` A52. **Completion notes (2026-08-07):** Implemented exactly as specified — `target_odds: float \| None = None` added to `MarketRecommendationOut`; `Pydantic.model_validate(market)` (unchanged call site) picks it up automatically, defaulting to `None` for a market dict with no such key. TDD: 2 new tests in `app/backend/tests/test_recommendations_schema.py` — non-null `target_odds` on a conditional market passes through unchanged; a market with no `target_odds` key at all defaults to `None`. Full backend suite: 259 passed. |
| W84 | completed | **Show the condition next to the Conditional/WAIT badge on the frontend.** `lib/types.ts`'s `MarketRecommendationOut` mirrors the new `target_odds: number \| null` field; `MatchUI.tsx`'s market adapter carries it into `MarketRec.targetOdds`; `ProbabilityRow` renders a one-line condition (e.g. "Needs 2.10+ to clear edge") under the market/selection name when `recommendationType === "conditional"` and `targetOdds` is non-null, using the same warning color (`STATUS_META.conditional.text`) already used for the badge. Decimal odds only, matching every other odds display in the app — no American-odds formatter exists anywhere in the codebase today, and this isn't the place to add one. **Acceptance:** a conditional market with a `targetOdds` shows the condition line; a conditional market with `targetOdds: null` (the ceiling case, or a pre-A52 cached row) shows no condition line and doesn't break layout; a `direct_bet`/`no_bet` market never shows one; component test covers all three. | Size S · Milestone M15 · Depends on: W83. **Completion notes (2026-08-07):** Implemented as specified, plus one addition beyond the story's literal text: the same `targetOdds`-driven condition (`wait ≥ {targetOdds}`) also replaced W84a's interim `min_odds`-based display on `MatchCard`'s collapsed footer, not just `ProbabilityRow` — the original ask that motivated W84a was about the collapsed Dashboard card specifically, and leaving it on the old unverified `min_odds` field while `ProbabilityRow` alone got the real fix would've left two different "wait" numbers coexisting in the app for the same market. `target_odds?: number \| null` added to `lib/types.ts`'s `MarketRecommendationOut` (optional, not required, so no pre-existing mock/fixture needed updating) and `MarketRec` (`MatchUI.tsx`); `applyRecommendation`'s adapter carries `m.target_odds ?? null` through. TDD: rewrote W84a's 3 `MatchCard` tests plus 1 new one to use `targetOdds` instead of `minOdds` (coherent value shows the line; `direct_bet` shows nothing; `null` shows nothing; absent/undefined — a pre-A52 cached row — shows nothing); rewrote the 1 `ProbabilityRow`/`MatchAnalysisPage` test plus 2 new ones (`null` and `direct_bet` show no condition line). Full frontend suite: 92 passed (11 files); `tsc --noEmit` clean. |
| W87 | completed | **Follow-up, direct user request (2026-08-07): W84's `wait ≥ {targetOdds}` text on `MatchCard`'s collapsed footer wasn't visually distinct from the rest of that line — same plain gray as the market/selection/day text around it, easy to miss and not clearly tied to the Conditional/WAIT badge's warning color.** Redesigned the collapsed card's ODDS box itself: for a conditional market with a real (non-null) `targetOdds`, the box shows `targetOdds` in `text-warning` (the same color as the Conditional badge) under a `"Wait ≥"` label, replacing the plain current-odds/`"Odds"` display outright — the current price is what already told the user this market isn't good enough yet, so surfacing it prominently alongside the real target would be noise, not clarity. Falls back to the normal current-odds/`"Odds"` box for every other case (`direct_bet`, `no_bet`, `null`/absent `targetOdds`), same convention as the pre-existing Result/Odds label swap for completed matches. `ProbabilityRow`'s condition text already used the warning color from W84 — unchanged. | Size XS · Milestone M15 · Depends on: W84. **Completion notes (2026-08-07):** `MatchUI.tsx`'s `MatchCard` ODDS box branches on `!isCompleted && shown?.recommendationType === "conditional" && shown.targetOdds != null`; the footer's redundant plain-text `wait ≥` suffix (added in W84) was removed now that the ODDS box conveys it more prominently. TDD: rewrote the 4 `MatchCard` wait-condition tests to check the ODDS box's label/value/color (`"Wait ≥"` + `text-warning`-classed value when applicable, plain `"Odds"` box otherwise) instead of a plain-text regex match; added a `text-warning` class assertion to the existing `ProbabilityRow` test. Full frontend suite: 92 passed (11 files); `tsc --noEmit` clean. **Found and fixed in the same pass, unrelated to the color-coding change:** `MatchUI.dashboardWindow.test.tsx`'s "labels asOf's own date 'Today'" test built its "today" fixture from `new Date().toISOString().slice(0, 10)` (a UTC date) while `dayDiff` compares via *local* Date getters outside sandbox mode — reproducibly failed for several real hours of every day (whenever local and UTC disagree on the calendar date, e.g. after 8pm EDT), the same UTC-vs-local mismatch class as W30/W48/W71. Fixed by building both fixtures from the exact same instant as `asOf` (and +24h for "tomorrow"), sidestepping the UTC/local distinction entirely rather than picking a "safer" hardcoded hour. |
