## PHASE 17: Per-League Data Freshness Display

> Found during a documentation catch-up pass (2026-08-07): `src/tools/data_tools.get_data_freshness()` gained a `by_league` breakdown under US#136 (`documents/user_stories.md` Phase 20) specifically because a single blended `is_stale` can mask one competition going stale behind another staying fresh — the motivating example in that story's own docstring is EPL sitting 58 days stale in its off-season while Sweden's fresh refresh keeps the blended top-level reading "fresh." `GET /api/status` (W17) already passes `by_league` through unmodified — it reached the frontend with zero backend work needed — but `AppShell`'s sidebar footer only ever rendered the blended figure, so the masking US#136 fixed engine-side was still fully present in the UI a bettor actually looks at.

| ID | Status | Description | Comments |
|---|---|---|---|
| W85 | completed | **Surface `data_freshness.by_league` in the sidebar footer, alongside the existing blended "Last Updated" line.** Only show a per-league breakdown once it's actually informative — a single-competition `by_league` (today: E0 only) is byte-for-byte the same number as the blended line above it, so showing both would be pure noise. **Acceptance:** with 0 or 1 `by_league` entries, the footer renders exactly as before (no visible change); with 2+ entries, one line per league appears below the blended figure, each independently colored/suffixed `-- stale` off its own `is_stale`, not the blended one — demonstrated by a case where the blended figure reads fresh but a specific league's own row still reads stale. | Size XS · Milestone M15 · Depends on: W17 (completed). **Completion notes (2026-08-07):** No backend change — `get_data_freshness()`'s `by_league` key (US#136) was already reaching `GET /api/status` unmodified; confirmed via `app/backend/tests/test_status_endpoint.py`'s existing pass-through test. `app/frontend/lib/types.ts`: `DataFreshness` split into a `LeagueFreshness` base type plus an optional `by_league: Record<string, LeagueFreshness>` (optional so a mock/response predating this story still type-checks unchanged). `AppShell.tsx`: `byLeagueEntries` derived from `data_freshness.by_league`, sorted by league code; rendered only when `byLeagueEntries.length > 1`, each row `{league}: {days}d ago -- stale` (or without the suffix), colored via the same `text-warning`/`text-ink-secondary` convention as the blended line, league code in its own `<span>` for reliable test targeting. TDD: 2 new tests in `AppShell.test.tsx` — one confirming a single-entry `by_league` renders no visible breakdown (regression guard for the "no change for today's E0-only reality" acceptance clause), one confirming a 2-entry `by_league` shows both leagues with independently correct staleness even while the blended top-level figure reads fresh (E0 58d-stale, SWE 1d-fresh, blended `is_stale: false`) — both failed correctly (query returned nothing / element not found) before the fix. Full frontend suite: 80 passed (11 files); `tsc --noEmit` clean. Backend suite unaffected (no backend files touched): 257 passed. |

## PHASE 18: Dashboard Always Shows the Next 10 Matches, Date-Grouped

> Requested directly (2026-08-07): a sandbox date with a genuine but small real matchday (e.g. 4 Premier League fixtures) still only showed those 4, since W46's forward-window widening (`documents/app_techspec.md` §9.10) only ever triggered when the same-day query came back *empty* — a non-empty-but-small day never qualified for the "next 10" view. Superseded, not layered on top of, W46's fallback-only behavior.

| ID | Status | Description | Comments |

## PHASE 20: Edge Display & Ranking Correctness

> Both found live via direct user report against the same screenshot session (2026-08-08), both root-caused to the same helper (`bestMarket()`, `MatchUI.tsx`) picking purely by numerically highest `value_edge` with no regard for whether that market was actually being recommended.

| ID | Status | Description | Comments |
|---|---|---|---|
| W90 | completed | **A "No Bet" card was showing a prominent, green-colored positive edge percentage** — a real, legitimate `no_bet` market can still have a positive `value_edge` (just below `min_value_edge`, or an A54-ineligible market), and `bestMarket()` picked whichever market had the numerically highest edge with no regard for `recommendation_type`, so a "No Bet" card could show e.g. `+3.2% EDGE` in the same green used for a genuine value bet. Direct user report: "the edge is 3%, it does not line up" against a card correctly labeled No Bet. | Size S · Milestone M15 · Depends on: none. **Completion notes (2026-08-08):** `bestMarket()` now filters to actionable markets (`recommendationType !== "no_bet"`) first, only falling back to ranking among all markets (including no_bet) when nothing is actionable at all — so a card with any real direct_bet/conditional market always shows *that* one, never an incidentally-higher-edge no_bet market instead. Both edge-color sites (`MatchCard`'s collapsed ODDS/EDGE box, and the expanded per-market `ProbabilityRow`) now also gate the green "good" color on `recommendationType !== "no_bet"`, not just `valueEdge >= 0` — a no_bet market's edge, even numerically positive, renders in the neutral color now, matching what the badge next to it already says. TDD: 2 new tests in `MatchUI.test.tsx` (an actionable market is shown/colored over a higher-edge no_bet one; a no_bet-only match's positive edge is not colored "good"). Full frontend suite: 100/100 passing (11 files), `tsc --noEmit` clean. |
| W91 | completed | **"Edge %" sort left every No Bet card in arbitrary (kickoff-derived) order relative to each other, instead of by the number actually shown on each card** — W90's fix to `bestMarket()`'s selection also changed `pricedEdge()` (shared by `rankTopEdges` and `sortMatches`, `lib/dashboardMetrics.ts`) to return `null` for any no_bet-only match, correctly keeping such a match from ever outranking a genuine bet in "Top Edges"/"Edge %" sort — but that also collapsed every no_bet match to the same excluded rank, so *within* that group the sort comparator had nothing left to compare and fell back to array order. Direct user report: three No Bet cards under "Edge %" sort read `+5.3%`, `+5.5%`, `+4.5%` in that literal, non-monotonic order. | Size XS · Milestone M15 · Depends on: W90. **Completion notes (2026-08-08):** `sortMatches`'s edge branch is now a two-tier comparator: `pricedEdge()` (real, actionable-only edges) remains the primary key, so a no_bet match still can never rank above a genuine direct_bet/conditional one regardless of its own number (W90's intent, unchanged) — but ties within either tier now break by each card's own `bestMarket()`-displayed edge, so a group of No Bet cards sorts by the numbers actually on screen instead of being left arbitrarily ordered. `rankTopEdges` needed no change — it already excludes anything with a `null` `pricedEdge()` outright, rather than ranking it, so it was never affected by the tie-order issue, only `sortMatches`'s "Edge %" toggle was. TDD: 2 new tests in `dashboardMetrics.test.ts` (multiple no_bet-only matches sort by their own displayed edge, not left tied in array order; a no_bet match still never outranks a genuine direct_bet even with a much higher displayed edge). ## PHASE 21: Scheduler Coexistence Under Real Concurrent Operation

> Motivated directly by Phase 23's own conclusion (`documents/user_stories.md`): the ML-engine's data refresh is now proven idempotent/recoverable/drift-aware in isolation, but nothing had tested what happens when it and the app's own EOD/T-30 recommendation scheduler are genuinely running at the same time against the same DuckDB file (`data/fpai_core.db`). **Confirmed empirically first, not assumed**: DuckDB enforces a real exclusive file lock — a second process opening *any* connection (even read-only) while a read-write connection is open elsewhere fails immediately with `duckdb.IOException`; it does not block/wait, and does not silently corrupt data.

| ID | Status | Description | Comments |
|---|---|---|---|
| W93 | completed | **The manual "regenerate now" endpoint (`POST /api/recommendations`) had zero handling for a `duckdb.IOException` from a concurrent scheduled data refresh** — an unhandled lock conflict would surface as a raw 500 to the user, unlike every other transient-external-condition path in this app (`_fetch_and_cache_fixtures`'s own `HTTPError`-to-503 precedent). | Size S · Milestone M16 · Depends on: none. **Completion notes (2026-08-08):** Investigated `_fetch_odds_for_manual_request` first and found it *already* has its own broad `except Exception` degrading to no-odds — a hypothetical `duckdb.IOException` from `HistoricalOddsClient`'s sandbox-mode DB read was already safe, nothing to fix there. `run_agent` (reaching into `data/fpai_core.db` via `ForecastService`) had no such protection at all. Added a targeted `except duckdb.IOException` around just that call, converting it to a clean `503` ("The match database is temporarily locked by a scheduled data refresh. Please try again in a minute.") — same pattern and wording style as the existing fixture-fetch precedent. TDD: `test_run_agent_hitting_a_locked_database_degrades_to_a_clean_503_not_a_raw_500` (`app/backend/tests/test_recommendations_endpoint.py`) — proved it has real teeth by temporarily catching the wrong exception type and confirming the real `duckdb.IOException` then propagates unhandled (reverted immediately after). Full suite: 1002 passed / 1 skipped, zero regressions. |
| W94 | completed | **Confirm, don't just assume, that the scheduled EOD batch's own already-broad exception handling (`eod_batch.py`'s `except Exception`) genuinely covers this same `duckdb.IOException` scenario too** — the existing `test_one_erroring_match_is_skipped_not_fatal` only ever exercised a generic `RuntimeError`. | Size XS · Milestone M16 · Depends on: W93. **Completion notes (2026-08-08):** No code change needed — confirmed live via a new, targeted test using the real exception type (`duckdb.IOException`, not a generic stand-in) instead of just trusting the existing broad-catch test's generic case generalizes. `test_a_locked_database_mid_batch_skips_only_that_match_not_the_whole_batch` (`app/backend/tests/test_eod_batch.py`) — one fixture's `run_agent` call raises `duckdb.IOException` mid-batch; confirmed the batch correctly marks only that one fixture `skipped` (not fatal to the whole batch) and still schedules T-30 for both fixtures, mirroring the existing generic-exception test's own assertions exactly. Unlike W93, this path turns out to already be safe by construction. Full suite: 1002 passed / 1 skipped, zero regressions (counted together with W93's own new test). |
| W95 | completed | **Should `run_refresh_data` itself retry-with-backoff on a transient lock conflict specifically, rather than its current log-and-reraise-and-wait-for-the-next-scheduled-fire behavior?** Confirmed live (W93/W94's own investigation) that a lock conflict is a real, clean, fast-failing exception (not a hang or corruption) — but `run_refresh_data` has no protection of its own against a *momentary* app-side read colliding with its own write step, and `run_refresh_job`'s existing log-and-reraise contract (`tests/test_data_refresh_scheduler.py`) means a single unlucky collision could silently skip an entire week's (or twice-weekly, for Sweden) refresh cycle rather than recovering within the same run. **Not yet decided:** whether a short in-process retry (the conflict is expected to be brief — the app's own DB touches are short, per-match calls, not held open for the whole refresh) is worth the added complexity versus the current simplicity of "wait for the next scheduled fire," given collisions are expected to be rare in practice (the ML-engine refresh's own connections, per US#154/US#155, are also short-lived, not held open across the whole multi-step chain). | Size S · Depends on: W93, W94. **Completion notes (2026-08-09):** Direct user decision: retry. Placed the retry at `DuckDBManager.connection()` (`src/utils/db_manager.py`) itself rather than in `run_refresh_data` or any individual call site — every DB operation in this codebase (the ML-engine's ingest/understat/fotmob/lineup/feature-rebuild steps, *and* the app's own reads) already routes through this one shared method, so fixing it there transparently covers all of them, including W93's own `run_agent` path (which now retries *before* ever reaching its 503 fallback — defense in depth, not a replacement for it). New `max_retries: int = 5`/`retry_delay_seconds: float = 1.0`/`sleep_fn` parameters (defaults chosen so a real collision, expected to resolve within a few seconds given every caller's connections are short-lived, rides out cleanly; `max_retries=0` preserves the exact prior fail-immediately behavior for any caller that wants it). `sleep_fn` injectable (mirroring `FootballDataClient`'s existing `_RateLimiter` precedent) so tests never wait on a real sleep. Confirmed empirically before writing anything (not assumed): DuckDB's lock is enforced **per-process**, not per-connection-object — two connections from the *same* process to the same file coexist fine, so a genuine collision can only be reproduced with a real second OS process, not simulated in-process alone. TDD: 4 new tests in `tests/test_db_manager_lock_retry.py` — 3 fast, deterministic tests mocking `duckdb.connect` directly (retries then succeeds once a simulated conflict clears, with the right number of injected sleeps; the original exception propagates once retries are genuinely exhausted; `max_retries=0` makes zero retry attempts) plus one real subprocess-based test (a genuine second OS process holds a real lock for ~2s; `DuckDBManager.connection()`'s real default retry/sleep rides it out and succeeds, confirmed by wall-clock elapsed time, not just a mocked assertion). Proved real teeth: temporarily reverted the default to `max_retries=0` and confirmed both default-dependent tests fail with the real, unhandled `duckdb.IOException` (including the real cross-process test), then reverted. Full suite: 1006 passed / 1 skipped, zero regressions. |

## PHASE 22: Sandbox Snapshot Staleness Detection (BUG-036)

> Third of three production-readiness sub-areas (after Phase 21's scheduler coexistence and `documents/user_stories.md` Phase 24's DB backup/restore). Direct structural gap left open by BUG-036 (`documents/bugs.md`): `SnapshotStore`'s sandbox replay-first behavior (`app/backend/recommendations.py`'s `_select_sandbox_snapshot_source`) trusts any on-disk `_complete.json` marker forever, with no way to know the recording predates a later fix to the underlying forecast pipeline. Found live twice in the same investigation session (2026-08-08) — a stuck cold-start SP1 card and 43 E0 cards stuck on a disproven 1-target forecast — both traced back to the exact same root cause: `config/model_selection.yaml` changed after the snapshot was recorded.

| ID | Status | Description | Comments |
|---|---|---|---|
| W96 | completed | **Detect a sandbox snapshot recorded under a since-changed `config/model_selection.yaml` instead of trusting it for replay forever.** Both known BUG-036 incidents traced to this one file changing (a model path landing in a consistent state, a broken path getting fixed) after the snapshot was already on disk — a general "did anything change" check would be speculative; this is the concrete signal both real incidents actually hit. **Acceptance:** a sandbox recording made under the current `model_selection.yaml` still replays normally (no behavior change for the common case); a recording made under a *different* `model_selection.yaml` content (including every pre-fix marker, which carries no hash at all) is treated the same as "not recorded yet" and triggers one fresh live re-record instead of silently replaying disproven data; the separate standalone `agent-snapshot` CLI corpus (`main.py`'s `run_agent_snapshot`, A11's historical/backtest determinism store) is explicitly untouched — that corpus is deliberately meant to stay pinned regardless of later model changes, unlike the live sandbox-serving path. | Size S · Depends on: none. **Completion notes (2026-08-09):** New `_current_model_selection_hash()` (`app/backend/recommendations.py`) fingerprints `config/model_selection.yaml`'s raw bytes (SHA-256, truncated to 16 hex chars, matching `agent_config_hash`'s existing truncation convention). `_run_agent_in_mode`'s record-mode marker write now stores `{"model_selection_hash": ..., "recorded_at": ...}` instead of a bare `"{}"`. New `_sandbox_marker_is_fresh()` gates the sandbox's own replay branch in `_select_sandbox_snapshot_source`: a missing marker, an unparseable one, one with no `model_selection_hash` field (every marker written before this fix), or one whose hash doesn't match the current file are all treated identically to "no recording exists," falling through to a fresh record pass. The corpus-bridge branch (W70, a separate, deliberately-pinned historical store) is untouched — still a bare `.exists()` check, by design. TDD: 3 new tests in `app/backend/tests/test_sandbox_agent_snapshot.py` (a stale-hash marker falls through to record mode; a pre-fix no-hash-field marker also falls through; a fresh record pass writes the current hash into the marker) plus the existing 16 tests in that file updated to write markers in the new shape (a fixed, monkeypatched hash, not the real repo file, so they don't depend on `model_selection.yaml`'s actual content). Proved real teeth: temporarily hardcoded `_sandbox_marker_is_fresh` to always return `True`, confirmed both new staleness tests fail with a real `AssertionError` (`['replay', 'live'] == ['record', 'live']`), reverted. Full backend suite: 280 passed, zero regressions. |

## PHASE 23: Deployment Readiness — Auth, CORS, and Reproducible Installs

> Direct user request (2026-08-09): planning a first-ever deployment (private test, ~5 days out) so other people can reach the app over the internet instead of just `localhost`. Investigated the real gaps directly rather than assuming: zero inbound authentication on any `/api/*` route (real cost risk — `POST /api/recommendations` triggers a paid LLM call), CORS hardcoded to `http://localhost:3000` (would silently reject the real deployed frontend's origin), and `requirements.txt` had no version pins at all (a fresh install on a deploy host could resolve different versions than the ones actually tested against).

| ID | Status | Description | Comments |
|---|---|---|---|
| W97 | completed | **Gate the API behind a shared-secret header once it's reachable from the public internet, and make the allowed CORS origin(s) configurable instead of hardcoded to localhost.** Both are prerequisites for a public URL existing at all — without the first, anyone with the link can run up the LLM/odds API bill; without the second, the real deployed frontend can't talk to the real deployed backend. **Acceptance:** local dev and every existing test is completely unaffected (both features off/default unless explicitly configured); once `APP_ACCESS_TOKEN` is set, every route except `/api/health` requires a matching `X-App-Token` header or returns 401, and that 401 still carries correct CORS headers (not a confusing header-less CORS error masking the real cause); `CORS_ALLOWED_ORIGINS` (comma-separated) controls the allowed origin(s), defaulting to the original `http://localhost:3000` if unset. | Size S · Depends on: none. **Completion notes (2026-08-09):** New `RequireAppTokenMiddleware` (`app/backend/main.py`) — checks `os.environ.get("APP_ACCESS_TOKEN")` per-request (dynamic, not baked in at import time, unlike CORS below), no-ops entirely when unset (same "off unless explicitly opted in" pattern as `ENABLE_SCHEDULER`), exempts `/api/health` and `OPTIONS` (CORS preflight). Deliberately added to the middleware stack *before* `CORSMiddleware` — confirmed empirically (not assumed) that Starlette wraps middleware in the reverse of `add_middleware()` call order, so adding `CORSMiddleware` *after* makes it outermost, meaning it intercepts real preflight requests before this check ever runs, and still attaches correct headers to a 401 this middleware returns. CORS origins now come from `CORS_ALLOWED_ORIGINS` (comma-separated) via a new `_parse_cors_origins()` helper, defaulting to the original localhost-only value; read once at import time like any other env-driven app config, not per-request. Frontend (`app/frontend/lib/api.ts`) gained a single `apiFetch()` wrapper (all 10 existing call sites now route through it) that attaches `X-App-Token` from `NEXT_PUBLIC_APP_ACCESS_TOKEN` when set — mirrors the backend's own off-by-default behavior. New `.env.example` documents every real env var this app reads (enumerated directly via grep across both `os.environ.get`/`process.env` call sites, not from memory) — for the hosting platform's env-var dashboard, not committed secrets. TDD: 9 new tests in `app/backend/tests/test_app_token_middleware.py` (unset-token no-op, missing/wrong/correct header, `/api/health` exemption, 401-still-has-CORS-headers, `_parse_cors_origins` parsing). Proved real teeth: temporarily short-circuited the token comparison (`if False and ...`), confirmed the 3 tests that actually exercise the check fail with a real `assert 200 == 401`, reverted. Full backend suite: 289 passed, zero regressions (up from 280). Frontend: 101/102 passing (1 pre-existing, unrelated cross-file flake in `MatchUI.dateboundary.test.tsx`, confirmed passing in isolation), `tsc --noEmit` clean. |
| W98 | completed | **Pin `requirements.txt` to exact versions instead of unbounded latest.** An unpinned install on a fresh deploy host can silently resolve different (potentially breaking) versions than whatever's been running and tested locally for months. | Size XS · Depends on: none. **Completion notes (2026-08-09):** Every top-level package in `requirements.txt` pinned to its actual currently-installed, currently-tested version (read directly via `pip show` per package, not guessed); `mcp` (listed but not actually installed in this venv — a separate, unrelated dependency of `src/mcp_server.py`, outside the app's own deploy path) deliberately left unpinned rather than fabricating a version number, with a comment explaining why. Verified via `pip install --dry-run -r requirements.txt` — zero resolver conflicts, only the pre-existing `mcp` gap would newly install. Full engine test suite unaffected (pins match what was already installed). |
| W99 | completed | **Fix `PersistingOddsClient.get_odds()` silently dropping the `date` argument (BUG-038), found live on the app's first-ever real deployment.** | Size XS · Depends on: none. **Completion notes (2026-08-09, found during live Railway deploy verification):** See BUG-038 (`documents/bugs.md`) for the full incident. `PersistingOddsClient.get_odds()` (`app/backend/scheduler_wiring.py`) now accepts and forwards `date: str | None = None`, matching `OddsAPIClient`/`HistoricalOddsClient`'s existing signatures — fixes both the manual "regenerate now" path (`main.py`) and the scheduled EOD batch's per-fixture-date fetch (`eod_batch.py`) from one shared fix, since both route through this same wrapper class in real (non-sandbox) production. New test `test_persisting_odds_client_forwards_date_to_the_inner_client`. Red-first verified: temporarily dropped the forwarding, confirmed a real `AssertionError`, reverted. Full backend suite: 290 passed, zero regressions (up from 289). Notable: this bug was structurally unreachable by any local/sandbox test or session, since local dev always runs `SANDBOX_MODE=1` (routes through `HistoricalOddsClient` instead) — only a genuine live, non-sandbox deployment ever exercises `PersistingOddsClient` at all, which is exactly why it surfaced only now, on the first real deploy. |
| W100 | completed | **One-time way to seed a deployed instance's persistent volume with the real `fpai_core.db` (168MB, deliberately gitignored) and `recommendation_cache.db`.** There's no reliable way to push a large local file directly onto a hosting platform's remote volume from a local machine, so the already-deployed, already-running app instead pulls one itself from a URL (e.g. a GitHub Release asset). | Size S · Depends on: none. **Completion notes (2026-08-10, direct follow-up to the first live deploy on Railway):** New `POST /api/admin/restore-database?target=...&source_url=...` (`app/backend/main.py`) — `target` is a fixed `Literal["fpai_core", "recommendation_cache"]` allowlist (not an arbitrary path, preventing path traversal or an unintended file write); `source_url` must start with `https://`; streams the download to disk in 8MB chunks rather than loading the whole file into memory. Already protected by W97's `RequireAppTokenMiddleware` like every other non-`/api/health` route — no separate auth code needed. TDD: 4 new tests in `app/backend/tests/test_restore_database_endpoint.py` (rejects a non-https URL, downloads+writes both allowlisted targets to the right path, rejects an out-of-allowlist target via FastAPI's own `Literal` validation). Red-first verified the https-only guard: temporarily disabled it, and the disabled version was caught not just by a failing assertion but by this repo's own real-network-call-blocking test safety net (`conftest.py`, W20) — proof the code would have actually attempted a live network call without the guard, not just a logical gap. Full backend suite: 294 passed, zero regressions (up from 290). |
| W101 | completed | **Manual trigger to run the ML-engine's own `refresh-data` chain against the deployed instance's data, closing the staleness gap left after W100's one-time snapshot upload (E0/SP1 79 days stale, SWE 22 days).** There's no cron/scheduler wired up for this yet on this deployment — `main.py`'s own `schedule-refresh` is a separate, never-deployed standalone process. | Size S · Depends on: W100. **Completion notes (2026-08-10):** New `POST /api/admin/trigger-data-refresh?league=...` (`app/backend/main.py`) — `league` is a fixed `Literal["E0", "SP1", "SWE"]` allowlist. Launches `main.py refresh-data --league <league>` as a genuinely separate OS process (`subprocess.Popen`, `cwd` set explicitly to the repo root), deliberately *not* an in-process function call: `main.py`'s own top-level imports include `mlflow` and other training-only machinery needed for its other subcommands, which would otherwise get pulled into this always-running web server process just by importing the file — exactly the class of avoidable memory bloat W100's OOM investigation (the memory-limit crash found the same session) confirmed isn't currently present in the server's own import graph. The subprocess carries and releases that memory on exit; the server's own footprint never grows. Returns immediately rather than blocking on the refresh finishing (can take several minutes of real scraping/xG-matching/lineup-fetching) — checking `GET /api/status` afterward shows when `data_freshness` reflects the update. Already protected by W97's `RequireAppTokenMiddleware`. TDD: 3 new tests in `app/backend/tests/test_trigger_data_refresh_endpoint.py` — correct command/league forwarded to a mocked `subprocess.Popen`, an out-of-allowlist league rejected via FastAPI's own `Literal` validation (never reaches `Popen`), and confirms `Popen` (not a blocking `run`/`check_call`) is used so the call doesn't wait for the subprocess to finish. Every test mocks `subprocess.Popen` deliberately — this repo's own network-blocking test fixture (`conftest.py`, W20) only patches `socket.socket` in the test process itself, which would *not* stop a real, un-mocked child subprocess from making real network calls. Red-first verified: temporarily swapped in a wrong CLI subcommand/argument shape, confirmed a real `AssertionError` showing the mismatch, reverted. Full backend suite: 297 passed, zero regressions (up from 294). |
| W103 | completed | **Turn on the scheduler for real (`ENABLE_SCHEDULER=1`), and pre-generate recommendations automatically on every startup so a freshly deployed/redeployed instance never shows blank "not yet generated" cards for near-term fixtures.** Direct user request, after seeing every card start blank on first load and only populate on click ("why are recommendations not shown first hand... whenever changes are made and triggers a redeployment, a refresh of recommendations should be triggered"). | Size M · Depends on: W08/W09/W10 (the scheduler infrastructure being turned on), W101 (established the admin-trigger-endpoint pattern this reuses). **Completion notes (2026-08-13):** New `_pregenerate_recommendations()` (`app/backend/main.py`) generates and caches recommendations for real upcoming fixtures (default: next 5 days) across every competition, reusing `eod_batch.run_eod_batch()`'s own bounded-concurrency/per-match-error-isolation machinery via its existing `fixtures=` override (the same mechanism W50's sandbox precompute caller already established) rather than a new mechanism — fetches fixtures via the same `get_fixtures()` the dashboard itself calls, groups by competition, runs one `run_eod_batch()` per league, and isolates one league's batch failure from the others. Two callers: `POST /api/admin/pregenerate-recommendations?days_ahead=...` (manual, independent of scheduler state) and a new hook inside `lifespan` that fires on every boot **gated behind the same `ENABLE_SCHEDULER` check as the scheduler registration itself** — deliberately not unconditional, since `ENABLE_SCHEDULER` persists as a Railway env var across every future redeploy once set, so gating there still satisfies "every future redeploy triggers a refresh" while keeping every test's `TestClient(app)` boot completely unaffected (the identical reasoning that made the scheduler registration itself opt-in, W08/W09). A `scheduler=None` case (manual trigger before the scheduler's ever been turned on) degrades gracefully — matches still generate and cache, just without a T-30 refresh registered for them, via a no-op `schedule_t30` closure instead of failing. New `_fire_and_forget()` helper (module-level `_background_tasks` set + a `add_done_callback` to discard) guards against `asyncio.create_task()`'s own documented gotcha — an unreferenced task can be silently garbage-collected mid-execution. TDD: 6 new tests in `app/backend/tests/test_pregenerate_recommendations.py` — fixtures correctly grouped into one batch call per league (not per fixture); graceful no-op degradation with no scheduler; a real scheduler gets a real `schedule_t30` wired through (`build_schedule_t30`'s own behavior, confirmed via `scheduler.schedule_once.assert_called_once()`); one league's batch failure doesn't block another's; the admin endpoint returns immediately without waiting for the (multi-minute) work; `_fire_and_forget` genuinely keeps and then releases its task reference. Red-first verified twice: (1) temporarily broke the per-league grouping (hardcoded every fixture into one league bucket), confirmed a real `assert 1 == 2` failure; (2) temporarily moved the pregenerate call *outside* the `ENABLE_SCHEDULER` gate to prove the gating itself matters — this didn't hang, but silently polluted 7 unrelated tests across `test_fixtures_endpoint.py` and this file's own admin-endpoint test with stray leaked background-task calls, concretely demonstrating the exact non-determinism class the gating comment warns about; both reverted after confirming. Full suite: 1050 passed / 1 skipped, zero regressions (up from 1044 — backend alone: 304 passed, up from 298). |

## PHASE 24: Per-Competition Display Toggle

> Direct user request (2026-08-13), during a live investigation of two Allsvenskan (SWE) cards both stuck on "Cold start — thin history" (feature_completeness 71.6%, high-entropy outputs). Root-caused to a genuine, permanent data-source gap (SWE's football-data.co.uk feed and its Understat xG backfill both structurally never cover Allsvenskan — see `documents/bugs.md` write-up alongside BUG-036/BUG-038), not a bug to fix. Given that and the big-5 leagues' season starting, the user asked to stop featuring SWE, and to make "turn a competition on/off" a reusable, config-driven switch rather than a one-off code change.

| ID | Status | Description | Comments |
|---|---|---|---|
| W104 | completed | **Add a `display_enabled` flag to the competition registry (`config/competitions.yaml`), and wire every place a competition is surfaced to users through it, so toggling one off/on is a one-line config edit.** SWE set to `display_enabled: false` as the first real use. | Size S · Depends on: none. **Completion notes (2026-08-13):** New `display_enabled: bool = True` field on `CompetitionDefinition` (`src/logic/competition_registry.py`) — unset key defaults to `True`, matching `available_targets`' existing "no key = no restriction" convention, so every pre-existing competition entry needed zero changes. New `list_display_enabled_competition_ids()` is the single read helper both real call sites use: `app/backend/main.py::get_fixtures()` (the dashboard's own fixture source, and — since W103's `_pregenerate_recommendations()` calls `get_fixtures()` directly — pregeneration too) now skips a disabled competition's client call entirely rather than fetching then discarding, so its odds/fixtures-API quota is never spent either; `app/backend/scheduler_wiring.py`'s nightly EOD/T-30 `_eod_job()` loop filters `COMPETITIONS` the same way. Deliberately scoped to *display* only, per the user's own framing — raw-data ingestion (`refresh-data --league SWE`) and the trained model/feature config are untouched, so re-enabling later needs no data rebuild, just flipping the flag back. TDD: 4 new tests in `tests/test_competition_registry.py` (default-true, explicit-false, `list_display_enabled_competition_ids` excludes a disabled one, and a real-registry guard asserting SWE is currently off — catches an accidental flip-back); 2 new tests in `app/backend/tests/test_fixtures_endpoint.py` and `app/backend/tests/test_scheduler_wiring.py` each (a disabled competition's client is skipped/never called; existing multi-competition merge tests updated with an autouse/explicit override back to "all on," mirroring the file's existing `sweden_client_mock`-style default-then-override pattern, since they test the merge mechanics independent of which competition is currently live). Full suite: 690 passed / 1 skipped (`tests/`), 306 passed (`app/backend/tests/`), zero regressions. |
| W105 | completed | **Follow-up to W104: a display-disabled competition was still visibly leaking through `GET /api/status`'s `by_league` breakdown (the `AppShell` sidebar's "Last Updated" per-league lines), even though it was already gone from the fixtures list.** Direct user follow-up ("No I do not want to see it in the app too"). | Size XS · Depends on: W104. **Completion notes (2026-08-13):** `get_status()` (`app/backend/main.py`) now filters `data_freshness["by_league"]` through the same `list_display_enabled_competition_ids()` from W104, before the response leaves the app. Deliberately scoped to just `by_league` — the only field that renders a literal competition label in the UI; the blended top-level freshness numbers (`latest_match_date`/`match_count`/`is_stale`) are left engine-wide (no label attached, so not a "seeing it" issue), and `src/tools/get_data_freshness()`/`get_model_status()` themselves are untouched — they're also consumed by the agent/CLI, which need the real, complete per-league picture regardless of what the app chooses to display (same "app filters, engine stays complete" split already used for `COMPETITION_ALLOWLIST`, `app_techspec.md` Section 4). `model_status`'s per-context model counts were checked too and left alone — no competition label is ever rendered from that field (`AppShell` only sums it into an anonymous count), and filtering it would have needed a real competition_id-keyed test fixture, unlike the existing `test_status_endpoint_returns_data_freshness_and_model_status` test's synthetic `"league"` key. New test `test_status_endpoint_hides_a_display_disabled_league_from_by_league`. Verified live against the real (unmocked) endpoint: `by_league` now returns only `{"E0", "SP1"}`. Full suite: 997 passed / 1 skipped, zero regressions. |
| W106 | completed | **Hide the "Bets" nav tab — direct user request, functionality not ready yet.** | Size XS · Depends on: none. **Completion notes (2026-08-13):** Removed the `Bets`/`/bets` entry from `AppShell.tsx`'s `NAV_ITEMS` array only — the route itself (`app/bets/page.tsx` → `BetTracker.tsx`, `active="bets"`) is untouched, so it's unlinked, not deleted; still directly reachable by URL, and flipping the nav entry back is the only step needed to re-surface it later. `AppShell.test.tsx`'s existing "renders nav links" test updated to stop asserting "Bets" is present; new test asserts it's absent. Full frontend suite: 103 passed (11 files), `tsc --noEmit` clean. |

## PHASE 25: Three-Persona UI Critique Follow-up

> Grew out of a UI critique (2026-08-13) of the current app from three user personas: someone with traditional sports-app experience (ESPN/Sofascore), someone with sports-betting-app experience (DraftKings/FanDuel/Bet365), and someone with no sports-app experience at all — only general social-media apps. Full critique is `documents/app_frontend_design.md`'s companion conversation, not reproduced here. Every story below is scoped, frontend-only, and needs no prior product decision — the bigger, decision-blocked findings from the same critique are tracked as D8–D11 in Open Design Discussions instead. **All UI-only** (no backend/API changes) — see the summary table at the end of this phase.

| ID | Status | Description | Comments |
|---|---|---|---|
| W107 | completed | **Add lightweight tooltips/inline explanations for betting jargon** (Edge, ML probability, Confidence, Cold Start Risk, Feature Completeness) **and status labels** (Direct Bet/Conditional/No Bet/Insufficient Data, BET/WAIT/PASS/NO READ). Sports-app-only and social-media-only personas have no way to interpret this vocabulary anywhere in the UI today — no info icons, no first-run explanation. | Size S · Depends on: none. **Completion notes (2026-08-13):** Native `title=` tooltips (`TrustSignal`'s existing pattern) added at every occurrence: `STATUS_META` gained a per-status `explain` string, applied to `StatusBadge` and the Match Analysis header's big verdict text; `TrustSignal`'s title expanded from a bare `feature_completeness=0.71` figure to a plain-language sentence plus the figure; new shared `EDGE_EXPLAIN`/`MODEL_PROBABILITY_EXPLAIN`/`CONFIDENCE_EXPLAIN` constants applied to every "Edge"/"Model"/"Confidence" label (`MatchCard`'s stat block, Model Probabilities table header, Match Analysis's Confidence line). Tier tag explanation and league-code expansion covered separately by W110 (same critique, split out since it already had its own label map to reuse). TDD: new `it.each` test asserts every `StatusBadge` status has a non-trivial `title`. Full frontend suite: 118 passed, `tsc --noEmit` clean. |
| W108 | completed | **Add a filter to show only actionable matches** (Direct Bet / Conditional) **on Dashboard and Match Explorer**, hiding No Bet / Insufficient Data. Betting-app-experienced users want a feed of only actionable edges; today the only controls are the Kickoff/Edge % sort (`SegmentedControl`, `MatchUI.tsx:449-474`), so non-actionable rows can't be hidden. | Size S · Depends on: none. **Completion notes (2026-08-13):** New shared `isActionable(match)` helper (`MatchUI.tsx`) replaces two previously-duplicated inline predicates (`activeEdgesCount` on both pages) and now also backs a new "Actionable only" checkbox on `DashboardPage` and `MatchExplorerPage`. Dashboard: filters before date-grouping, with its own "No actionable matches right now" empty state; the Edge Distribution/Top Edges rail and Active Edges count stay computed over the full loaded set regardless of the filter (a display concern for the list only). Match Explorer: applied after the existing team-name search in the same `useMemo`. TDD: new tests in `MatchUI.dashboardWindow.test.tsx` and `MatchUI.dateboundary.test.tsx` — mock a mixed actionable/non-actionable result set, toggle the checkbox, confirm hide/restore. Full frontend suite: 118 passed, `tsc --noEmit` clean. |

Betting-app-trained users click `LogBetButton`'s "Log bet"/"Confirm" (`MatchUI.tsx:973-1036`) expecting a real-money bet slip; today's only disclaimer ("Bets you've actually placed — not automatic hypothetical tracking," `BetTracker.tsx:293`) is invisible from that inline flow. | Size XS · Depends on: none. **Acceptance:** the tracking-not-placement framing is visible everywhere "Log bet" can be triggered (`MatchCard`'s expanded row, Match Analysis's `ProbabilityRow`, and the manual entry form), not only on `/bets`. |

## PHASE 27: Daily Edges Clarity — Date Grouping & Match Card Hierarchy (Feedback)

> Direct user feedback (2026-08-13) on the Dashboard ("Daily Edges"): which date a match belongs to isn't clear, and there's no clear separation between date groups; separately, within each `MatchCard`, visual priority should read **Team → Market + Odds (for each direction) → Selection + Edge → days until kickoff**, each with an explicit visual cue — not the current mostly-textual left-to-right ordering.

| ID | Status | Description | Comments |
|---|---|---|---|
| W116 | completed | **Make each date group on Daily Edges visually unambiguous** — a real separator/heavier header between groups, not just the current small uppercase muted label + count pill (`DashboardPage`, `MatchUI.tsx:812-830`). Direct feedback: "it should be more clear which Date the matches belong to and there is no clear separation between each date." | Size S · Depends on: none. **Completion notes (2026-08-13):** Header text upgraded from `text-xs ... text-muted` to `text-sm font-bold ... text-ink`, with its own `border-b border-border-strong pb-2` giving each group a real dividing line; inter-group spacing widened `gap-6` → `gap-8`. Pure styling change over `groupByDate()`'s existing, unchanged grouping. Full frontend suite: 118 passed, `tsc --noEmit` clean. |
| W117 | completed | **Reorder `MatchCard`'s visual hierarchy to Team → Market + Odds (each direction) → Selection + Edge → days until kickoff, with explicit visual cues for each.** Today's collapsed card (`MatchUI.tsx:563-649`) leads with kickoff time + tier tag, shows only the single "best market" (`bestMarket()`) rather than odds for every direction, and folds the day-until-kickoff label into a plain text string rather than giving it its own visual treatment. `MatchCard` is shared by both Dashboard and Match Explorer, so this reorder applies to both. | Size M · Depends on: none. **Completion notes (2026-08-13):** New `marketDirections(match, marketName)` (`MatchUI.tsx`) returns every row sharing the recommended market's own name (e.g. a result_3way's home/draw/away rows). Card rebuilt top-to-bottom: (1) team row, now first; (2) a compact tier-tag/status sub-header (supplementary metadata, not one of the four ordered tiers); (3) a new per-direction odds board — only rendered when there's more than one direction (a single-selection market would just repeat the number the row below it already shows) — the recommended direction gets an accent border + bold text, every other direction shows its raw price only, deliberately never its edge (transparency, not a second recommendation); (4) the existing Selection+Odds/Result+Wait≥+Edge block, unchanged in logic, market/selection text no longer has the day appended; (5) a new days-until-kickoff badge (day + kickoff time as separate text nodes, not one interpolated string — needed so "today"/"tomorrow" stay exact-text-matchable) plus W113's odds-source caption. TDD: one pre-existing test's premise flipped intentionally (a higher-edge no_bet direction's price is now *supposed* to show, just never its edge or the recommended styling) and was updated, not just patched to pass; 3 new tests cover the multi-direction board, the single-direction suppression, and the day badge's independent findability. Full frontend suite: 118 passed, `tsc --noEmit` clean. |

| W130 | completed | **Bug report: "the scrollable went through the top banner"** — direct follow-up screenshot on W129, showing a scrolled date-group header rendering above/through the sticky "Daily Edges" title instead of staying cleanly hidden behind it. | Size XS · Depends on: W129 (fixes a regression in it). See [[BUG-046]] for the full root-cause writeup. **Completion notes (2026-08-14):** Root-caused live with Playwright rather than guessed at — launched the dev server, mocked fixtures spanning several days to force real scrolling, scrolled `<main>` directly, and inspected actual `getBoundingClientRect()`/computed styles. Found `<main>` (`AppShell.tsx`) carried its own `pt-8` while also being the `lg:overflow-y-auto` scroll container — padding on a scroll container sits inside its scrollport, so scrolled cards could slide up through that 32px strip (sticky title top = 95px vs. `<main>`'s own top = 63px, a 32px gap nothing opaque covered). Fix: moved `pt-8` off `<main>` onto a new wrapper `<div>` rendered inside it around `{children}` — not the scroll container, so its padding scrolls away with the content instead of staying exposed; `<main>`'s true top is now 0-padding, so `lg:sticky lg:top-0` headers land flush against it. Re-verified the same live repro after the fix (title's rect.top now equals main's rect.top exactly; screenshot shows clean coverage), plus the unscrolled state and a real iPhone-13-profile mobile screenshot both visually unchanged. New `AppShell.test.tsx` regression test pins the structural invariant (`<main>` itself never has `pt-8`; its first child does) since jsdom can't exercise real sticky/scroll paint order. Full frontend suite: 130 passed, `tsc --noEmit` clean (the one known pre-existing flake in `MatchUI.dateboundary.test.tsx` re-confirmed unrelated — passes clean in isolation). |
| W131 | completed | **Bug report: "the part with 'Daily Edges' and the right panel still goes up by a little bit before turning stationary. Also the scrolling feels laggy."** Direct follow-up screenshot on the very next scroll after W130 shipped. | Size XS · Depends on: W130 (fixes a side-effect of it). See [[BUG-047]] for the full root-cause writeup. **Completion notes (2026-08-14):** W130's fix (moving `<main>`'s `pt-8` onto an inner wrapper so it wouldn't leak through the sticky title) traded away a property the broken version had for free: padding *directly on the scroll container* doesn't count as travel toward `position: sticky`'s `top: 0` threshold, but padding on a normal descendant does — so post-W130 the title/rail sat 32px below `<main>`'s true top and only "stuck" after 32px of real scroll, a visible settle-jump each time. Verified live with Playwright by sampling `getBoundingClientRect()` at 1px/5px/15px/32px of scroll (title didn't reach `<main>`'s top until 32px). Fix (`MatchUI.tsx`): `lg:-mt-8` on `DashboardPage`'s outer content row cancels the wrapper's `pt-8`, so the row's natural position is already flush with `<main>`'s true top; `lg:pt-8` added back onto the title and rail individually so their own opaque backgrounds still cover that space (keeping W130's fix intact) and the visual spacing is unchanged. Re-verified live: title's `top` now equals `<main>`'s own top at every sampled offset from 1px through 100px, with unscrolled/scrolled screenshots matching pre-bug spacing and coverage. Separately checked for a genuine perf issue (not just the visual snap) via a CDP trace of a real wheel-scroll — near-zero script/task duration, no evidence of layout thrashing — so "laggy" is most likely this same jump; user asked to recheck live now that it's gone. Full frontend suite: 130 passed, `tsc --noEmit` clean (the one known pre-existing flake reconfirmed unrelated). |
